### Scene 8 — the fan kid recognizes his idol — [How To Direct an AI Short Film Like a Filmmaker (Full Workflow + Prompts) — "Santiago"](Santiago-breakdown.md) P24
- Source: https://higgsfield.ai/blog/Santiago-breakdown | Model: Seedance 2.0 4K | Settings: one continuous crane-up shot

```text
Style: 8K IMAX. Photorealistic — Documentary style. Cinematography: Emmanuel Lubezki × Roger Deakins. Color: 60:30:10 — dominant / secondary / accent. Camera: ARRI Alexa, physical cine lens, handheld throughout — strong real operator presence: breath, body-weight shifts, sway, constant micro-drift and live reframes, slightly looser and more restless than locked handheld, no static shots, NOT digital jitter. 180° shutter motion blur. Skin: Pore-level realism — pore-shadow matching on-set light. Acting: Hollywood — micro-pauses before reactions, precise eye-line, wet living eyes with catch-lights, visible breath and chest rise, a smile that builds gradually. Physics: Gravity and inertia respected — mass has real weight, the ball held with real weight, correct contact shadows. No floating props. Composition: Rule of thirds + golden ratio — ASYMMETRIC framing in EVERY beat, subjects pushed off-center (left or right third), never centered, never symmetrical; varied headroom and lead-room shot to shot. Continuity: Characters, props, environment identical across every cut. No identity drift. Technical: 24fps smooth motion, all action in real time. 8K detail. No jitter. SUBJECT — @fan-kid (matches input 100%), a 10yo boy in football kit, bright, eager, breathless from running. To one side of frame, @santiago (matches input 100%), adult male, dark curly hair, light stubble, holding worn football @street-ball in both hands — kept as a silhouetted torso/hands only, silent, no face, no reaction. WB 5500K. ONE CONTINUOUS SHOT — the boy's gratitude → silent recognition → rising joy → an eager plea. LOCATION — @city-street STYLE REFERENCE ONLY, not a fixed keyframe. Daytime city street, warm soft daylight, light haze. Model extends the world; subjects move through space, NOT pinned to the input frame. ACTION — the boy runs up for his ball, thanks Santiago, freezes recognizing his idol, bursts out he's a fan, then begs to be taught. 0:00–0:03 — @fan-kid runs up toward @santiago , breathless, gaze fixed on @street-ball in Santiago's hands; still looking at the ball he says: "Thank you, sir!" 0:03–0:06 — he lifts his gaze to Santiago's face and takes the ball into his own hands with real weight — then freezes, a stunned silent beat, recognition of his idol landing. 0:06–0:10 — flustered, eyes shining, he asks unsure with a rising tone: "Uhh— you're... Santiago?" — a pause — then it lands and he bursts out with certainty: "I'm your fan! You're the best!", excitement building, chest rising. 0:10–0:15 — glowing, he steps closer, clutching the ball, and pleads eagerly: "Please— teach me your tricks!", bouncing on his feet with hope. Hard cut. CAMERA — ARRI Alexa, ONE continuous handheld shot with live internal movement, no cuts. Begins as an extreme LOW worm's-eye angle almost on the asphalt, tight on @street-ball and the running feet of @fan-kid approaching; as the boy arrives and takes the ball, the camera floats UP and CRANES with him in one unbroken restless move — rising from ground level up to a low-then-eye-level framing on the boy's lit-up face, drifting in closer as he speaks. @santiago stays a soft dark silhouette edge (torso/hands) on the extreme left foreground, cutting into frame, never his face. Strong operator breath and sway, live reframes, dutch tilt on the rise for an off-balance feel; boy held hard off-center, NEVER centered. STYLE — Dominant warm amber 60% / secondary city grey-blue 30% / accent the boy's kit + the ball 10%. WB 5500K. Soft even daylight, low sun raking the lens for pronounced optical flares and gentle halation; fine airborne dust particles drifting and catching the light; light atmospheric haze softening the depth. Catch-lights in the eyes, no hard key. CONSTRAINTS — 21:9. ONE CONTINUOUS SHOT, NO cuts, the camera never cuts by itself. NO slow-motion; all motion in real time at a single consistent frame rate, NO time-lapse or sped-up crowd. Handheld throughout with a live crane/float from ground level up to the boy's face — restless operator movement, NO static shots, NOT digital jitter. Only the boy speaks; @santiago stays SILENT and appears ONLY as a silhouetted foreground edge — NO face, NO reaction shot, at no point turned to camera. No eye glow, no watermark, no subtitles. [AUDIO] NO MUSIC. SFX ONLY — diegetic sound and live audio throughout. No score, no soundtrack, no background music of any kind. STYLE: 8K photorealistic, anamorphic widescreen, original sea-kaiju blockbuster grade, fine grain, hyperdetailed. LIGHTING: cold deep-ocean blue-green key with surface god rays from above; hot bioluminescent glow from the creature as accent; bright overcast surface daylight when breaching; volumetric light through water; lens water-droplets refracting. COLOR: deep teal-and-black ocean as the dominant, the whale-leviathan's dark mottled hide and reflective eye as the saturated subject, glowing bio-light as accent; murky, no clean color. CAMERA: anamorphic cine optics, real motion blur, register shifts — slow eerie macro on the eye, then a HARD FAST ZOOM into the wild eye, explosive on the breach, water-on-lens on the dive, weighty in the fight. Never static. MATERIAL: material-level realism — wet barnacled whale-like hide, a huge glossy reflective eye with detailed iris, slick cephalopod tentacles, refracting water, foam, droplets clinging and streaking on the lens. ACTING: the leviathan's eye is shut, then SNAPS open with a wild, alert glare; the creature surges up, breaches, dives back, and grapples the cephalopod — the whole fight stays in and on the water, never lifting into the air. PHYSICS: true mass and fluid dynamics — the breach displaces a wall of water and spray; the dive-back collapses water inward with foam, droplets hitting the lens; tentacles whip with weight and drag; the grapple churns bubbles and particles. Both creatures stay at the water surface — they thrash, roll, and submerge, but never fly or lift fully out of the sea. CHARACTER DESIGN (original, not based on any franchise):LEVIATHAN: a colossal whale-like sea-beast, dark mottled barnacled hide, a huge glossy reflective eye with a pale wild iris and dark pupil, broad fins, a long finned tail, faint bioluminescent veins.CEPHALOPOD: a colossal deep-sea cephalopod, mottled red-brown skin, many long suckered tentacles, a bulbous mantle, large dark eyes. CONTINUITY: SAME leviathan eye and body, SAME cephalopod, SAME ocean across all cuts. The eye that opens in CUT 1 is the exact same eye zoomed into in CUT 2. The fight stays at the sea surface from start to finish. TECHNICAL: 24fps, slow-motion on the breach only, ultra high detail, motion accelerating from stillness into violent surface combat. AUDIO: diegetic only — muffled deep-ocean ambience, a rising rumble as the eye opens, a sharp sting on the eye-snap, a colossal water-burst on the breach, rushing spray, a heavy plunge on the dive, churning water and tentacle whips, two titanic roars, bubbles, subsonic boom.SCENE CONTEXT A colossal whale-leviathan's eye snaps open in the dark deep; a hard fast zoom hits its wild glare; it surges up and breaches the surface, dives back (water on the lens), and battles a colossal cephalopod — the entire fight staying in and on the water.FORMAT MODE: Sequence of 6 cuts, no timecodes. OPTICS: CUT 1 — ECU 18° macro, the closed eye. CUT 2 — ECU 14° HARD FAST ZOOM into the eye as it snaps open. CUT 3 — WS 63° slow-motion breach from just above the sea. CUT 4 — MS 47° handheld dive-back, water on lens. CUT 5 — WS 63° surface grapple. CUT 6 — EWS 84° wide on the churning surface fight. ACTION: CUT 1 — extreme macro: the leviathan's eye is shut in the dark water, still, bio-veins faintly pulsing. CUT 2 — the eye SNAPS open and a hard fast zoom punches in on its wild, alert glare — pale iris, dilating pupil, the same eye in sharp detail. CUT 3 — slow-motion: the leviathan breaches, exploding up through the surface in a wall of water and spray. CUT 4 — handheld: it dives back down, water crashing inward, droplets hitting and streaking across the lens, the frame foaming for a beat. CUT 5 — at the surface: the cephalopod lunges, tentacles whipping and coiling around the leviathan; they grapple, thrashing half-in the water. CUT 6 — wide just above the waves: the two colossal creatures roll and thrash in a storm of spray, foam and bubbles, then submerge together. Hold. Hard cut to black. POSITIVE LOCKS: SAME leviathan eye and body, SAME cephalopod, SAME ocean every cut. The closed eye in CUT 1 is the exact eye that snaps open and is zoomed into in CUT 2 — hold the detailed wild glare. The fight STAYS at the sea surface throughout — the creatures breach, dive, grapple and submerge, but NEVER fly or get carried up into the air. Deep teal dominant; the leviathan and its eye are the saturated subjects.
```

### Observational courtroom documentary sequence (faces hidden) — [How to Make Documentary Videos with AI in 2026](ai-documentary-videos-2026.md) P1
- Source: https://higgsfield.ai/blog/ai-documentary-videos-2026 | Model: Cinema Studio 4.0 (prompt written by Supercomputer) | Settings: 30 s, 16:9, six shots, Drama/2020s/Calm, Modern camera, Clean Sharp, f/1.4, Soft Cross, 720p

```text
SCENE CONTEXT
A 30-second observational documentary courtroom sequence, 16:9, six shots. An elderly defendant in a wheelchair is brought slowly into a courtroom hiding his face behind a raised blue folder; masked officials position him at the dock, the robed judges enter, and the presiding judge asks a single question by microphone. Every face in the video stays hidden — behind the folder, behind blue medical masks, or too far away to read. Shot like a patient vérité documentary: long takes, honest handheld, available light, no graphics, no music.
CHARACTERS
DEFENDANT: a very elderly man in a wheelchair — dark wool overcoat over a grey cardigan, a dark felt brimmed hat, thin liver-spotted trembling hands. His face is NEVER visible: for the entire video he holds a blue cardboard folder raised in front of his face, only the hat brim showing above it. Voice: very old, thin, quiet, slightly hoarse.
ESCORT LAWYER: a man around 40 in a navy suit, white shirt, blue medical mask covering nose and mouth; he pushes the wheelchair slowly and stays beside the dock.
COURT OFFICER: a woman in a dark uniform, blue medical mask, holding the courtroom door.
JUDGES: three judges in plain black robes, entering in file and sitting at the bench. They wear NO masks — and for that reason they exist only in wide shots, always at a distance where their features stay soft and unreadable. PRESIDING JUDGE: the center figure, grey-blonde hair tied back, black robe. Voice: a measured, even female voice carried through the courtroom microphones.
GALLERY: a sparse public and press gallery at the rear seen only as the backs of heads and shoulders, blue medical masks visible on every partial profile; one press photographer near the door appears only as a silhouette with a camera. No face of any person is ever readable anywhere in the video.
LOCATION MAP
A generic modern European courtroom, no identifying features: tall dark wooden double doors in the left wall; a parquet floor; cream-plastered walls with beige curtains and tall windows giving soft daylight along the far side; a low white partition and clear plexiglass dividers separating the dock area center-left — a wooden desk with a gooseneck microphone and a small water cup; the long dark wooden judges' bench across the far end with document folders, microphones and plexiglass panels between seats, and a private door in the wall behind it; rows of public benches at the rear. No flags, no coats of arms, no emblems, no name plates, no readable documents or signs anywhere.
FORMAT MODE
Six shots, five HARD CUTS at 3.5s, 7.0s, 15.0s, 18.5s and 23.5s. Observational documentary grammar: unhurried takes, every cut changes camera position, geography stays constant; no dissolves, no transitions, no speed changes.
OPTICS
S1 = detail lens on the door hardware, shallow focus, corridor melting soft beyond. S2 = detail at floor level on the wheels, shallow focus, parquet grain sharp. S3 = wide standard lens, deep focus across the room. S4 = wide from the rear gallery, deep focus over the backs of heads. S5 = true wide holding the entire judges' bench small in frame. S6 = full-room wide from the rear corner holding both the dock and the distant bench in one composition, deep focus. Natural spherical perspective throughout.
CAMERA
Patient observational handheld in every shot — a documentary operator breathing with the room: soft sway, slow honest reframes, no hurry, no artificial jitter, subjects never lost. S1 and S2 hold close and almost still. S3 pans slowly right with the wheelchair at its own unhurried pace. S4, S5 and S6 hold their wide compositions with living stillness.
ACTION TIMING
0.0s–3.5s — S1, DETAIL, the door. Close on the dark wooden door's brass handle and hinge line: the latch turns softly, and the tall heavy door swings open with a long, low creak — the masked COURT OFFICER's uniformed shoulder and hand guide it; beyond the opening, the soft unfocused shape of the wheelchair waits in the corridor light. CUT as the door reaches open.
3.5s–7.0s — S2, DETAIL, the wheels. Floor-level shot on the parquet: the wheelchair's grey rubber wheels roll slowly over the threshold into the room, spokes turning without hurry, a faint squeak on the seam, the escort's polished shoes stepping patiently beside; the boards carry soft reflections of the window light.
7.0s–15.0s — S3, WIDE, the crossing. From inside the room the whole entrance breathes: the officer at the door, the DEFENDANT rolled in slowly behind his raised blue folder, hat brim above it, the masked ESCORT LAWYER pushing at a walking pace with no haste at all; a photographer's silhouette lingers by the door with two soft shutter clicks; the camera pans gently right with them as the wheelchair crosses the parquet, rounds the low partition and eases behind the plexiglass toward the dock desk. The roll takes the full eight seconds.
15.0s–18.5s — S4, WIDE from the rear gallery over the backs of masked spectators' heads. The lawyer swings the wheelchair into place at the dock desk, sets the brake with a click, bends the gooseneck microphone toward the folder and settles onto the chair beside; the folder stays perfectly raised; the room murmurs faintly and quiets.
18.5s–23.5s — S5, WIDE on the judges' bench, the whole bench small in frame. The private door behind it opens; the three robed JUDGES file in one after another — unmasked, their faces soft and unreadable at this distance — take their places, set down papers, and at 22.3s sit almost together, robes settling; the PRESIDING JUDGE at the center draws her microphone closer.
23.5s–30.0s — S6, FULL-ROOM WIDE from the rear corner of the gallery: the dock with the raised blue folder mid-left, the judges' bench small across the far end, the plexiglass and daylight holding the whole room in one still composition. At 24.3s the presiding judge leans to her microphone and asks, even and clear through the speakers: "Can you hear me?" A long documentary pause — the room utterly still — then at 26.6s the DEFENDANT's thin, aged voice answers through the desk microphone: "Yes." — and the blue folder dips a single centimeter with the word. The wide room holds in silence; one distant camera shutter clicks at 28.5s; room tone breathes to 30.0s. End.
PHYSICS
The heavy door swings with real mass and a genuine slow hinge creak. The wheelchair rolls with true rolling resistance on parquet — soft rubber murmur, spokes turning at believable slow speed, one small squeak, a firm brake click. The blue folder is held by genuinely old hands: a constant faint tremor, never perfect stillness, but it never lowers. Robes, coats and masks move as real cloth; the plexiglass catches faint natural reflections; chairs and benches take weight with quiet creaks.
LIGHTING
Available-light realism, constant across all shots: soft daylight from the tall curtained windows mixing with warm ceiling fixtures; gentle natural falloff, mild practical hotspots on the wooden bench; no cinematic shaping, no dramatic contrast, no glow or bloom. The plexiglass panels pick up faint window reflections. The honest, unshaped light of a real room.
IMAGE LOOK
Observational documentary cinematography on a modern digital cinema camera: neutral true-to-life color, ordinary honest contrast, crisp but unstylized detail, natural motion blur, the faintest sensor noise in the shadows. No film grain, no stylized grade, no vignettes, no chyrons, no graphics, no watermarks.
AUDIO
One continuous diegetic soundscape, no music anywhere. Courtroom room tone — ventilation hum, distant shuffles, a sparse cough. 0.3s–3.0s: the latch and the long low door creak, featured close and dry; 3.5s–7.0s: the wheels rolling on parquet, featured — soft rubber, a faint squeak on the seam, patient footsteps; 7.0s–15.0s: the slow crossing — wheels, steps, cloth, two soft camera shutters, the room's quiet attention; 15.5s: the brake click and the gooseneck adjusting with a low thump through the PA; 19.0s–23.0s: the bench door, robes, papers, chairs taking weight; 24.3s: the presiding judge through the speakers, even and clear: "Can you hear me?"; 26.6s: the thin aged voice on the desk microphone: "Yes."; then held room silence, one distant shutter click at 28.5s, room tone to the end. These two lines are the only spoken words in the entire video.
POSITIVE LOCKS
No human face is ever visible or readable at any moment of the video: the DEFENDANT's face stays fully behind the raised blue folder in every frame — the folder never lowers below his eye line and no blur effect is used or needed; every other person near the camera wears a blue medical mask or is framed from behind; the unmasked JUDGES appear only in wide shots at a distance where their features never resolve, and are never framed in a medium, close or detail shot. Exactly one blue folder, one wheelchair and one defendant exist throughout. The two scripted lines are the only dialogue, spoken exactly as written. The courtroom stays generic: no flags, coats of arms, emblems, name plates, chyrons, captions, watermarks or readable text anywhere. Geography, wardrobe and light never change; the camera stays patient observational handheld from the gallery side of the room.
```

```text
A young woman sits at a wooden table by a large sunlit café window, lifts her cappuccino, takes one slow sip while gazing outside, and lowers the cup as a soft smile forms. Low golden morning sun through the window is the only light. Steam rises from the cup. CHARACTER. The woman, the only foreground character: early twenties, fair skin, long light-brown hair with a middle part falling loose past her shoulders, wearing a cream ribbed knit sweater. Calm, unhurried, quietly content; lips closed except the sip. Her face, hair and sweater stay identical in every frame. LOCATION MAP. Cozy plant-filled café interior, morning. Camera is inside the room at seated eye level, looking along the window wall. Screen-left: the large window spanning x 0% to 30%, wood-framed panes with fine condensation droplets on the glass, low sun blazing through blurred greenery outside. Midground left: lush green trailing plants hanging above and beside the window. Center: the wooden table edge running along the window sill. Deep background right: a café bar with shelves, glassware and a barista working, all softly defocused; blurred patrons at tables in the mid-depth behind the plants. FIRST FRAME AND SPATIAL BLOCKING. The first visible frame already contains the full staging — no empty establishing frame, no delayed reveal. Opening pose: she sits at x 60%, y 50%, in right-facing three-quarter profile turned toward the window, the cup raised in her right hand at x 45%, y 62%, her eyes down on the coffee, a thin ribbon of steam rising; her left hand rests on the saucer on the table at x 44%, y 88%. Her gaze path: from the cup, up and out through the window screen-left; she holds that outward gaze to the end. She never looks at camera. The sun flares at the upper-left window corner, x 5%, y 10%. FORMAT MODE. Single continuous take, 8 seconds, real-time speed. No cuts, no transitions. OPTICS 29° diagonal field of view, short telephoto portrait lens character, camera about 4 meters away — close framing through lens reach, not proximity. She is razor-sharp; the plants, patrons and bar dissolve into creamy soft bokeh; the window blooms. Locked for the whole shot. CAMERA Nearly static with a barely perceptible slow drift-in over the full 8 seconds — a breathing tripod frame, no handheld shake, no pans. Chest-up medium close in three-quarter profile, she sits right of center with the window and flare occupying frame-left. Focus locked on her face; the window may bloom but never steals focus. ACTION TIMING 0.0s to 1.0s — Full staging from frame one: she looks down at the raised cup, steam curling, her thumb settling on the handle. 1.0s to 3.5s — She lifts the cup to her lips and takes one slow sip, eyes rising to the window mid-sip; her throat moves as she swallows. 3.5s to 6.0s — She lowers the cup to upper-chest height; a soft genuine smile forms and reaches her eyes; the low sun models her cheek and catches flyaway hairs. 6.0s to 8.0s — She settles the cup down toward the saucer with both hands cradling it, gaze still out the window, the smile resting easy. She blinks once, slowly. The frame breathes and holds. PHYSICS The cup has real weight: small wrist corrections keep it level, the liquid tilts gently, the sip tips it with true inertia, ceramic meets saucer with a soft settle. Steam rises only from the cup, bending in still air and brightening where it crosses the sunlight. Her hair shifts subtly as her head turns; the knit sweater creases naturally at the elbows. Dust motes drift in the sunbeam. LIGHTING. One light source: low golden morning sun through the window camera-left, warm amber key raking across her face in three-quarter backlight — bright rim on her hair and cheek, the window side of her face fully modeled, the camera side lifted only by soft bounce off the table and pale interior, shadows open with visible detail, never crushed. Warm haze and gentle halation around the window panes, a soft horizontal flare from the sun corner, condensation droplets on the glass sparkling as bokeh. The interior reads dim and warm against the bright blooming window; nothing oversaturates, skin keeps true texture with visible pores. AUDIO. Quiet café room tone: soft espresso-machine hiss far behind, low murmur of patrons, a gentle ceramic clink as the cup settles, one quiet sip. No dialogue, no subtitles, no score. POSITIVE LOCKS. Her identity stays locked for all 8 seconds — same face, hair, sweater in every frame, no drift. She stays in right-facing three-quarter profile and never turns to camera. Steam exists only above the cup; the air at her mouth stays clear. The window is the only light source for the full shot. The take is one unbroken, nearly static drift with focus locked on her face. Kodak Vision3 250D texture: fine organic film grain, gentle halation on the window highlights, filmic highlight roll-off, warm photochemical palette of amber, honey and deep green. Lubezki-style natural-light realism — a real film frame, not digital video.
```

### STRONG motion/light prompt — torch-lit forest walk — [How to Make AI Video Look Real in 2026: Lighting, Motion and Camera Control](ai-video-look-real-2026.md) P4
- Source: https://higgsfield.ai/blog/ai-video-look-real-2026 | Model: Seedance 2.5 / Kling 3.0 / Cinema Studio | Settings: 10 s single take, 84° wide, handheld retreat, torch-only key

```text
A lone man walks straight toward the camera through a dark twilight forest, sweeping a burning torch in his right hand as he inspects the dark ahead. Every move of the torch visibly changes the light on his face, while a faint cold blue-hour glow shapes the forest behind him. The shot is a study of one moving flame against dying twilight.
LOCATION MAP
Dense forest at deep blue hour, minutes after sunset. Camera retreats backward along a rough forest path, facing the man. Foreground: the flame, thin drifting smoke, and one blurred fern at the frame edge. Midground: the man walking toward camera over uneven ground of roots and leaf litter. Background: black vertical tree trunks layered into depth, thin ground mist between them, and gaps in the canopy showing a deep twilight-blue sky gradient, darkest overhead, faintly brighter low behind him. The torch is the only warm light; the sky gives only a weak cold ambient.
FIRST FRAME AND SPATIAL BLOCKING
The first visible frame already contains the man walking with the torch burning and the blue twilight visible behind him; no empty establishing frame, no delayed reveal.
The man: mid-thirties, lean and weathered, dark rugged expedition clothing, alert and focused. Frontal, midground at x 50%, y 55%, framed chest-up, torso facing camera, walking straight toward the lens at a slow steady pace, eyes scanning the darkness past the camera.
The torch: gripped in HIS RIGHT hand, which appears screen-left. Flame starts at head height, x 38%, y 40%, about 40 cm from his face. The flame stays inside the frame for the entire shot.
Because the flame starts on his right side, the right side of his face is lit warm and the left side falls into shadow with only a trace of cool blue edge — this left-right logic must stay physically correct all shot.
Foreground occlusion: a dark blurred fern at FG lower-right, x 88%, y 85%, heavily defocused for the full shot.
Background: tree trunks as blue-black silhouettes between x 10% and x 90%, sky gaps glowing dim twilight blue in the upper third.
FORMAT MODE
Single continuous take. Real-time speed. No cuts, no transitions, no speed ramps.
OPTICS
84° diagonal field of view, classic wide-angle lens character, camera 1.2 to 1.5 meters from the man. Strong but natural perspective, straight lines rectilinear, no fisheye curve. His face is sharp and dominant; the flame blooms soft; the forest recedes into layered blue-black depth. Subtle horizontal flare streak from the flame, gentle optical vignette.
CAMERA
Handheld, eye-level, frontal. The operator walks backward at the man's exact pace, holding the 1.2–1.5 meter distance for all 10 seconds: shoulder-mounted mass, visible breathing sway, soft footstep pulse, small human corrections — organic and alive, never digital jitter, never gimbal-smooth. Focus stays locked on his face for the entire shot; the flame may bloom but never steals focus.
ACTION TIMING
0.0s to 2.5s — Full staging from frame one. He walks toward camera, torch raised beside his right cheek at x 38%, y 40%. The right side of his face glows warm and bright; the left side sits in shadow with a faint cold blue trace; the dividing line runs down his nose. Firelight flickers on his skin in rhythm with the flame.
2.5s to 4.5s — He extends his right arm forward to inspect something ahead: the flame moves toward the camera, growing larger in frame at x 40%, y 45%, and AWAY from his face — the warm light on his face visibly drops and he dims toward a cool blue-edged silhouette against the twilight, while nearby trunks and mist flare warm.
4.5s to 7.0s — He sweeps the torch in an arc across his body to his left side: the flame crosses frame from x 40% to x 62%. As it passes his face the light surges bright for a beat, then the lighting flips — now the LEFT side of his face is lit warm and the right side falls to shadow with its faint blue edge. Long tree shadows rotate through the mist opposite the flame's travel.
7.0s to 8.5s — He pulls the torch back in close, within 25 cm of his left cheek at x 55%, y 38%: light intensity on his skin jumps to its brightest, pores and sweat visible, both eyes catching warm glints, the blue ambient disappearing from his face entirely.
8.5s to 10.0s — Still walking, he lowers the torch to chest height at x 45%, y 58%: the face is now underlit from below, brow and nose shadows invert upward, the top of his head falls to cold blue-black. He keeps moving toward camera, breath fogging warm through the glow.
PHYSICS
Light on the face obeys the flame physically at every moment: closer flame means brighter skin, farther flame means dimmer skin, and the lit side of the face is always the side the flame is on — warm light falls off fast with distance, and where it dies, only the faint blue ambient remains. The flame behaves like real fire: it leans against the arm's motion, stretches on fast sweeps, flutters in night air, recovers when the arm settles; embers detach and drift upward; heat shimmer bends the air above it; thin smoke trails the moving flame. The torch has mass — his wrist and forearm visibly carry and steer it with acceleration and deceleration. His steps land with real weight: leaf litter compresses, twigs snap, roots shift his balance. Ground mist stirs as he passes. Breath vapor drifts in the cold air.
LIGHTING
Two light sources with a strict hierarchy. KEY: the hand-held torch flame — warm orange-amber, flickering, moving; the entire lighting of his face is driven by the flame's position, distance and height, changing continuously and naturally. AMBIENT: faint blue-hour skylight, many times weaker than the torch — a cold blue wash that only separates tree silhouettes from black, breathes a dim gradient into the sky gaps, and leaves the thinnest cool edge on his shoulders, hair and the shadow side of his face; it never fills his features, never competes with the flame. The shot is exposed for the flame: the forest holds deep crushed shadow, no fill light beyond the sky's trace, no flat front light. Halation blooms around the flame; the mist carries warm glow near him and cold blue farther away.
AUDIO
Close torch crackle and flame whoosh on every sweep, footsteps pressing into leaf litter, an occasional twig snap, low night wind moving the canopy, one distant owl call, his controlled breathing. No dialogue, lips still, no subtitles, no score.
POSITIVE LOCKS
The torch is the only warm light for all 10 seconds and the light on his face tracks the flame position at every frame; the blue ambient stays faint and cold. Focus stays locked on his face for all 10 seconds. He walks toward camera and the camera retreats with him for the full duration. Kodak Vision3 500T tungsten texture: dense organic film grain, pronounced halation around the flame, crushed blacks with a soft toe, photochemical palette of deep orange against deep twilight blue. IMAX-scale clarity on his face, Panavision-style anamorphic character: oval bokeh and subtle horizontal flare. Lubezki-style natural-light handheld realism — the frame looks like a real archival film shot, not digital video.
```

```text
A young man sits on a chair in a dim room at blue-hour dawn, coffee mug in hand, then rises and walks to the window to watch the world outside. The soft cold light from the window is the main illumination, growing on him as he approaches, while the room rests in soft, open shadow that keeps visible detail. Steam rises only from the mug.
CHARACTER
The man, the only character in the shot: early-to-mid twenties, deep skin tone, lean athletic build, very short cropped hair, thin mustache with light chin stubble, small stud earring, thin gold chain over a white tank top, open red-and-black plaid flannel shirt, baggy light-blue jeans, silver watch on his left wrist. Calm and heavy with thought; his lips are closed and stay closed except for one sip. His face, skin texture, hair, wardrobe and jewelry stay identical in every frame.
LOCATION MAP
Dim quiet interior room at blue-hour dawn. Camera is inside the room, facing across it toward the window. Screen-left midground: a simple wooden chair beside a small table where the man sits in the first frame, deeper in the room's shadow. Right side of frame: the window — divided panes, a thin curtain edge, faint condensation on the lower glass, a soft blue-grey overcast sky and blurred landscape outside. Between chair and window: two to three meters of open wooden floor, his walking path. The rest of the room is dark but readable: pale walls, a shelf, the shapes of furniture all hold soft visible texture in the shadow — nothing dissolves into pure black. Foreground: a softly blurred furniture edge at frame-left.
FIRST FRAME AND SPATIAL BLOCKING
The first visible frame already contains the full staging — the man seated on the chair, mug in hand, a thin ribbon of steam rising from the coffee; no empty establishing frame, no delayed reveal.
Opening pose: seated on the wooden chair at x 30%, y 56%, body angled screen-right toward the window, elbows on knees, the mug in his right hand at x 34%, y 68%, steam curling up from it. His eyes are already on the window light.
His path: he rises and walks screen-right along the open floor to the window, arriving in profile at x 45%, y 52% in the final framing, face turned to the glass, eyes fixed on something far outside. He never turns toward camera at any point — seated, walking and standing he stays in profile-to-three-quarter.
The window: right third of frame, spanning x 62% to 95%, y 15% to 75%, divided panes, curtain edge at x 60%.
Foreground occlusion: softly blurred furniture edge at FG left, x 6%, from y 55% to the bottom of frame, defocused for the full shot.
Light logic: the farther he is from the window, the dimmer he reads; the window side of his face and body brightens step by step as he approaches, and the camera side always sits in gentle open shadow that still shows his features clearly. This stays physically correct all shot.
FORMAT MODE
Single continuous take. Real-time speed. No cuts, no transitions, no speed ramps.
OPTICS
47° diagonal field of view, standard normal lens character, locked for the whole shot — the framing change comes only from the camera physically moving. Natural human-eye perspective, zero distortion, natural face and body proportions. He is sharp; the window blooms gently; the room stays soft but legible behind him. Gentle optical vignette; a subtle horizontal flare off the window at the closest framing.
CAMERA
Handheld and alive for the full 10 seconds — the operator is a physical presence in the room. The shot opens at the man's seated eyeline, camera 3.5 meters away; as he stands, the camera RISES with him in one organic motion; as he walks, the operator walks too, tracking alongside and slightly behind with a visible footstep pulse, breathing sway, and small human reframes that keep him composed against the window; the distance closes naturally to about 2 meters. In the final two seconds the operator plants and the frame settles into a medium close profile, chest-up, with a soft residual sway that never fully freezes. Shoulder-mounted mass throughout — organic and alive, never digital jitter, never gimbal-smooth. Focus stays locked on his face for the entire shot; the window may bloom but never steals focus.
ACTION TIMING
0.0s to 2.5s — Full staging from frame one: he sits in the dim depth of the room, mug in hand, steam rising from the coffee, eyes on the window. He leans forward, plants his feet, and rises off the chair — the chair creaks, his weight settles onto his legs. The camera rises with him.
2.5s to 5.5s — He walks unhurried to the window: three or four soft steps across the wooden floor, the mug carried level at chest height, the steam ribbon trailing and bending with his motion. With each step the cold window light grows across his face and the flannel. The camera tracks him, footsteps and breath alive in the frame.
5.5s to 8.0s — He arrives and settles into profile at the glass. He raises the mug and takes one slow sip; his throat moves as he swallows; steam from the mug drifts up through the window light past his chin. The camera eases off its walk and drifts into the final framing.
8.0s to 10.0s — He lowers the mug back to chest height, lips closing, jaw settling; his eyes never leave the world outside. The window glow models his brow, nose and lips, the gold chain picking up a soft cool highlight, the camera side of his face clearly readable in soft open shadow, one faint catchlight in his visible eye. Steam rises only from the mug in the lower frame; the air at his mouth and nose stays clear. He blinks once, slowly. The frame breathes and holds.
PHYSICS
Standing has real weight: feet plant, knees and hips drive up, mass settles, the chair shifts and creaks behind him. His steps land with true weight transfer on the floorboards. The mug has weight; he carries it level with small wrist corrections, the liquid tilting gently inside; on the sip his wrist tips it with real inertia. Steam behaves like real vapor and comes only from the coffee: it rises from the mug, trails behind it during the walk, bends in the still room air, brightens crossing the window light — and never appears at his mouth or nose; the room air is warm and his breath stays invisible, lips closed except the sip. The open flannel drapes and swings slightly with his steps; the chain shifts on his chest. The handheld frame carries the operator's real body: rise, steps, breath, settle.
LIGHTING
One light source: soft diffuse blue-hour daylight through the window — cool grey-blue, heavily softened by the overcast sky and thin curtain. It is the key light, and distance shapes it: seated, he sits deeper in the room in dim open shadow; as he walks, the window light strengthens across him step by step; at the glass, it models the window side of his face fully. It wraps: natural bounce off the pale walls and floor gently lifts the shadow side of his face and the whole room, so every shadow stays open with full visible detail. The cool light sits naturally on his deep skin tone — soft specular sheen on the brow, cheekbone and nose bridge — and mutes the red plaid to a desaturated slate-red; nothing oversaturates. Low-contrast, soft-gradation image: the darkest areas are deep grey with texture, never pure black — no blackout zones, no crushed shadows, no hard contrast edge, the falloff across his face is long and gentle. Exposure balances window and interior: the panes read bright and softly blooming while the room reads dim but fully legible. No lamps, no warm sources, no flat front light. Gentle halation around the window panes and along his lit profile edge.
AUDIO
Quiet room tone, faint muffled early-morning ambience beyond the glass, one soft chair creak as he rises, three or four unhurried footsteps on wood, the soft ceramic shift of the mug in his hand, one quiet sip and swallow. No dialogue, lips still and closed, no subtitles, no score.
POSITIVE LOCKS
The man's identity stays locked for all 10 seconds — the same face, skin texture, cropped hair, thin mustache, stud earring, gold chain, plaid flannel, white tank top, baggy jeans and silver watch in every frame; no identity drift as the framing tightens. Steam exists ONLY above the mug: no vapor or smoke ever appears at his mouth or nose at any point, his lips stay closed except the single sip, and the air around his head stays clear in every frame. The window is the only light source, its strength on him growing naturally as he approaches, and its soft bounce keeps all shadows open and detailed for all 10 seconds. He stays in profile-to-three-quarter and never turns to camera — seated, walking or standing. The shot is one unbroken handheld take: the camera rises with him, walks with him, and settles at the window without ever going static or gimbal-smooth. Focus stays locked on his face for all 10 seconds. ### Plant-goddess vs three friends — timecoded multi-shot adherence test prompt — [Which AI Video Tools Are Best at Following Your Prompt?](ai-video-prompt-adherence-comparison.md) P1
```
- Source: https://higgsfield.ai/blog/ai-video-prompt-adherence-comparison | Model: Seedance 2.5 / Cinema Studio 4.0 / Veo 3.1 / Gemini Omni Flash 1.1 | Settings: 15 s multi-shot, 4K 24fps look, 5 image refs, timecoded shots + negatives

```text
Global. ARRI Alexa 35, 4K, 24fps, 180° shutter, Kodak Vision3 250D film emulation, fine grain,
subtle anamorphic flares. Light: bright afternoon, hard sun at 40° from behind-right, 5600K key,
cool 7000K sky fill, bounce off facades and asphalt, deep blue sky one stop hot; green-pink
bounce entering the shadows in the second half, cold steel speculars in the finale. Every cut
lands on a motion match — the camera always moves in the same screen direction across the
edit, so nothing snaps.
IMPORTANT — CLEAN PLATE RULE (every single shot): absolutely no logos, no brand marks,
no emblems, no badges, no hood ornaments, no manufacturer names, no license plates, no
stickers, no decals and no text of any kind on any vehicle — all cars and taxis are plain, generic,
unbranded shapes with smooth bare bodywork and blank grilles. Equally, no logos, no brand
marks and no text on any footwear — all shoes and boots are plain unbranded, no side stripes,
no swooshes, no heel tabs, no printed soles, no visible tags. No readable text anywhere else in
frame either — no shop signs, no street-sign lettering, no billboards, no banner text, no graffiti.
Every surface that would normally carry writing is blank.
IMPORTANT — STAGING RULE: from 06.3 onward the three friends stand in front of her, facing
her, squared up shoulder to shoulder in the middle of the roadway; she is straight ahead of them
down the avenue and they never turn away. The main confrontation coverage is a wide shot
from behind their backs — their three small silhouettes in the foreground, her enormous figure
filling the avenue ahead of them. The camera works around this axis but never crosses it.
Guy 1 (<<<image_1>>>): Asian man, grey knit beanie, dark fringe, brick-orange zip-up track
sweatshirt with racing patches, wide light blue jeans, plain black sneakers with no branding.
Guy 2 (<<<image_2>>>): white man, dark green cap, knitted rugby polo in green-yellow-cream
stripes with white collar, olive wide shorts, white socks with green stripes, plain black loafers
with no branding.
Guy 3 (<<<image_3>>>): Asian man, messy dark hair, burgundy mesh tee over grey hoodie,
camo cargo pants, plain tan boots with no branding, lime green backpack on one shoulder.
Location (<<<image_4>>>): wide sunlit New York avenue, brick and stone high-rises, plain
unbranded yellow taxis and generic cars with completely blank bodywork, no plates and no
markings, traffic lights, street lamps, wide sidewalks with pedestrians, street receding toward
distant skyscrapers.
Creature (<<<image_5>>>): a 45-meter plant goddess, taller than the buildings — her crown
level with the 20th floor. Extremely slender, narrow-hipped, elongated build: thin stem-like limbs,
no wide hips, straight willowy silhouette. Smooth sage-green sculptural skin with soft gradients,
serene closed-lidded face, small pink star mark by one eye, dark green leaf-plate bodice and
layered leaf skirt, leaf pauldrons at the shoulders. Crown of tall crimson-pink bud petals standing
upright like flames, with white and pink blossoms nested among them. Pink petals constantly
drifting from her body. Movement slow, weightless, almost floating. Style: hand-painted stylized
3D, soft shading, deliberately illustrated against the live-action city.
00.0–02.2 | Sidewalk. The three friends walk along the sidewalk, talking, glancing around, Guy 2
pointing up at a building, Guy 1 laughing, pedestrians passing. Cam: 35mm T2.8, chest height,
Steadicam reverse tracking front-three-quarter, matched to their pace, medium three-shot. Light:
hard rim sun on shoulders and beanie, faces in soft facade bounce.
Cut on the camera's leftward drift.
02.2–03.4 | Onto the road. Profile side view as they step off the curb into the middle of the
roadway — all three stop dead, a deep rolling boom of enormous footsteps rolling down the
avenue, heads turning to look straight up the street. Cam: 40mm T2.8, side tracking with them,
decelerating to a stop as they freeze, one micro-jolt in the lens. Light: shadows starting to
tremble.
Cut on the jolt.
03.4–04.2 | Windows. Glass rattling in the high-rise frames, sky reflections rippling, dust shaking
off the sills. Cam: 85mm T2.0, low angle up the facade, slow rise, vibration in the mount, shallow
DOF. Light: sun snapping off the shaking glass in short specular flashes.
Cut on the rise — next shot continues descending.
04.2–05.0 | Cars. Plain unbranded taxis and generic cars rocking on their suspension, hazards
blinking, mirrors trembling, sun skating across smooth bare hoods — blank grilles, no emblems,
no plates, no text anywhere. Cam: 24mm T4, curb level low on the wheels and suspension, slow
descent to the ground, handheld shake. Light: hard specular on paint, deep cool shadow under
the chassis.
Cut on the descent — next shot is already at ground level.
05.0–06.3 | The sprout. Guy 3 crouches slightly, looking down; the asphalt cracks and a small
green flower pushes through, unfolding pink petals. Cam: 100mm macro T2.0, at ground level,
slow orbit around the flower plus a gentle push-in, razor-thin DOF, his plain unbranded boots
and the street soft behind. Light: raking backlight translucent through the petal, green
subsurface glow.
Cut on the orbit — next shot continues the same rotational direction.
06.3–07.4 | Squaring up. The three of them stand shoulder to shoulder in the middle of the road,
facing straight down the avenue at her, heads tilting back, pupils dilating, lips parting. Cam:
50mm T2.0, profile medium shot at eye level, arc continuing around them while tilting up on their
eyeline, handheld breathing. Light: the sun goes behind her crown — key drops two stops, faces
falling into cool shadow.
Cut on the tilt — next shot continues upward.
07.4–09.4 | The standoff — wide from behind their backs. Wide shot from directly behind the
three of them: their small dark silhouettes stand together in the middle of the roadway in the
lower foreground, backs to camera, heads tilted up — and ahead of them, filling the avenue
between the high-rises, the slender goddess walks toward them, taller than the buildings, thin
stem legs passing between the towers, crimson bud crown eclipsing the sun, pink petals drifting
down the street toward the three men. Cam: 18mm T5.6, low behind-the-back position on the
confrontation axis, continuous slow push in toward her past their shoulders with a gentle tilt up,
strong converging verticals, handheld breathing. Light: full backlit eclipse, petals glowing
translucent, god rays fanning between them, her long shadow stretching down the avenue over
the three of them, dust and pollen in the air.
Cut on the push — next shot continues forward, now high above her.
09.4–11.2 | High behind her shoulder. High angle from above and behind the goddess, close
over her shoulder and crown: her upright crimson bud petals fill the foreground, her narrow back
and leaf pauldrons below, and far down the avenue between her thin shoulders the three tiny
figures stand in the middle of the road, facing up at her. Petals drift up past the lens. Cam:
40mm T4, elevated behind-the-shoulder position, slow forward creep along her walking direction
with a gentle downward tilt, light handheld shake, her crown occasionally clipping the frame
edge. Then crash zoom in: snap the lens rapidly down the avenue past her shoulder onto the
three figures, very fast and punchy, keeping them readable through the sudden scale change,
landing on a bold tight composition of the three of them from her point of view. Light: raking sun
rimming her crown, flare across the frame, hard anamorphic streak on the zoom, focus lost and
regained.
Cut on the zoom's landing — next shot starts tight at ground level and pulls out.
11.2–12.4 | Grass at their feet. Tight on their plain unbranded shoes planted on the asphalt:
grass and small pink flowers pushing up between the cracks, spreading fast around their soles.
The camera pulls back and rises to their faces as they glance down, then at each other — none
of them breaks the line, they stay turned toward her. Cam: 35mm T2.8, low, fast pull-back and
crane up, handheld. Light: strong green bounce filling the shadows, warm sun patches breaking
through her crown.
Cut on the crane — next shot continues rising.
12.4–15.0 | The armor. Back to the wide shot from behind their backs, now closer: the three of
them exchange a nod and drop into fighting stances shoulder to shoulder, still facing her,
holding the middle of the road, her enormous figure towering ahead of them down the avenue.
Dark grey matte steel armor materializes over them in a single fast upward sweep —
segmented boots, shin and thigh plates, articulated gauntlets sliding over the fingers, back and
shoulder plates snapping into place with a metallic ripple, closed full-face helmets sealing last.
Their street clothes vanish beneath the plating; each suit carries a thin painted trim in its owner's
color — brick-orange, dark green, burgundy. Pink petals fall around them throughout. Final
frame: three dark grey armored figures braced in a row from behind, facing her, her silhouette
filling the sky ahead. Cam: 32mm T2.8, low behind-the-back position, continuous rise into a slow
push-in, handheld breathing settling to almost steady on the last beat, slight roll. Light: cold hard
speculars racing across the fresh steel as each plate forms, backlit sun through her crown
haloing the three armored silhouettes, god rays between the petals.
Negative: drone flyby, aerial fly-through, smooth cinematic drone, car logos, car emblems, car
badges, brand marks, hood ornaments, manufacturer names, license plates, number plates,
stickers, decals, taxi markings, shoe logos, sneaker branding, side stripes on shoes, printed
soles, heel tabs, shop signs, street sign text, billboards, banner text, graffiti, any readable text,
watermark, characters facing away from the creature, running away, photorealistic flower, wide
hips, curvy hourglass figure, morphing faces, extra limbs, duplicate characters, distorted
architecture, oversaturated, warped hands, plastic skin, static camera, human-scale creature,
sexualized design, glowing neon sci-fi armor,
```

```text
SCENE CONTEXT: Five friends around a small campfire on a grassy coastal bluff, late blue-hour dusk. They agree to meet more often. Real-time, about 15s, five segments, four hard cuts. CAST, five distinct people, no repeated face or wardrobe: P1 man 32, dark-blond, light grey henley. P2 man 29, dark curls, stubble, dark green shirt-jacket. P3 woman 27, long straight black hair, olive field jacket. P4 woman 30, wavy copper hair, cream cable knit. P5 woman 26, ash-blonde low bun, navy cardigan. Exactly five people. No twins, no duplicated wardrobe, no extra figures. BLOCKING: Fire foreground center, x50 y74, blurred. Group in a semicircle beyond it, 1.5 m from flames. Camera stays on one side of the fire; the 180 axis runs through the fire and is never crossed. P3 and P4 stay screen-left looking camera-right; P1, P2, P5 stay screen-right looking camera-left. First frame already shows three people seated in position, no empty establishing frame. OPTICS AND CAMERA: Seg 1 and 5, 47 degree FOV, camera 3.5 m, group two/three-shot. Seg 2 and 4, 29 degree FOV, camera 4.5 m, close-up with a blurred listener's shoulder in the near foreground. Seg 3, 47 degree FOV, camera 3 m, medium close-up. No lens drift inside a segment. Shoulder-held at seated eye level, slow operator breath, shutter 270-360 degrees, natural motion blur. No zoom, no push-in, no digital jitter. BEATS: 1. Group wide. P1 to P3: "I don't know why we let a whole year go by." Hard cut. 2. P3 close-up, eyes on P1 off-frame right: "Then let's stop letting it." Hard cut. 3. P4 medium close-up, mug in hands: "Same fire, same people. Once a month." Hard cut. 4. P5 close-up, three-quarter left, sparks crossing frame: "I'm holding all of you to that." Hard cut. 5. Back to P1 with P2 behind: "Then it's a plan." Long held static final beat. Only the named speaker talks; listeners keep lips closed. No offscreen voices. PHYSICS: Fire burns like real wood, fast irregular tongues, tips breaking off, uneven height, thin smoke drifting one way. Sparks rise on turbulent curved paths and fade, never in straight lines. Light sea wind moves loose hair and smoke consistently. Bodies carry weight, mugs tilt with liquid inertia. LIGHTING: Key is the fire itself, 2200-2700K, from below. Fill and rim from cold 6500K dusk sky, separating each person from the dark ridge. Firelight swells slowly and organically with the flame shape, shadows rolling off gradually. No high-frequency strobing, no stepped luminance jumps, no unmotivated source. Warm catchlight in every eye. AUDIO: Only the five scripted English lines, close and dry. Continuous wood crackle, quieter on close-ups, soft wind and distant surf. Ambient ducks under speech. No music, no subtitles. LOOK: Photoreal, shot on ARRI Alexa 35, natural grain, warm fire against cold blue dusk, visible pores, freckles, fabric weave. Same faces, wardrobe, seating order, fire size and light direction across every cut. No text, no logos, no watermarks.
```

```text
SCENE CONTEXT: Rally special stage on a wet gravel forest road, Nordic pine woods, low fog, overcast dusk. One rally car runs the stage. Real-time, about 15s, seven segments, six hard cuts. SUBJECT LOCK: One rally car only, white and blue livery, round door number panel, mud-caked flanks, headlights on. Right-hand drive, steering wheel on the right in every exterior and interior shot. Driver in helmet with closed dark visor and gloves, co-driver right of frame in the cockpit. Livery pattern, mud level and light state stay identical across all cuts. SCREEN DIRECTION LOCK: The car travels right-to-left in every exterior shot. Camera stays on one side of the road; the 180 axis never flips. Fog density and road surface stay continuous across cuts. BEATS: 1. Wide static, 47 degree FOV, car exits a bend right-to-left, headlight cone on wet gravel. Hard cut. 2. Wide, tighter framing and lower angle, car hits a standing puddle, fine dispersed water and mud droplets burst outward and fall fast, thin spray that clears within half a second, silhouette of the car never hidden by a solid wall of mud. Hard cut. 3. Cockpit medium, camera on the roll cage, driver right of frame at the right-hand wheel, wipers sweeping in rigid synchronous mechanical arcs across muddy glass, rain beads streaking. Hard cut. 4. 29 degree FOV side tracking pan, car right-to-left, background streaked with motion blur, car sharp. Hard cut. 5. Extreme close-up, 84 degree FOV fender-mounted, tread biting wet gravel, stones and mud flicking off the tyre. Hard cut. 6. Wide static, car launches over the crest right-to-left, a fast clean parabola, apex reached in under half a second, immediate descent, heavy landing with suspension compressing hard, gravel and spray kicked out on impact, car continues out of frame. No hovering, no mid-air pause, no slow motion. Hard cut. 7. Bonnet POV, 84 degree FOV, road rushing forward through fog, final static held beat as the road straightens. PHYSICS: Full gravity and inertia at all times, mass loads the outside suspension in the bend, the body rolls and settles, tyres deform under load. Water breaks into droplets, never into a frozen solid cloud. Mud lands and sticks, spray thins as it rises. Wipers are rigid mechanical arms with a fixed pivot, no bending, no morphing. LIGHTING: Overcast 6000K daylight through fog as key, soft and directionless. Headlights as the only warm practical, cutting a visible cone in the mist. No unmotivated sources. AUDIO: Turbocharged four-cylinder engine, real gear changes and anti-lag pops. Distinct impact sounds, puddle slap on beat 2, engine rising as the wheels leave the ground on beat 6, hard suspension slam and gravel scatter on landing. Engine level drops with distance and loses high end when the car is far or out of frame. No music. LOOK: Photoreal, shot on ARRI Alexa 35, natural grain, cool teal-green forest grade, wet gravel and mud texture, condensation on glass. No text, no logos beyond the car livery, no watermarks.
```

```text
First frame: full whiteout inside the cloud — soft volumetric grey-white mist filling the frame edge to edge, faint brightness from above, thin vapor streaks rushing past the lens with the camera's speed. 0–4s: the camera pushes forward and slightly downward through the cloud mass, wisps whipping past, light pulsing brighter and dimmer as density changes. 4–6s: the cloud base tears open — ragged curtains of vapor part around the lens and the landscape resolves below in one continuous reveal. 6–12s: the full establishing vista: the canyon running away from camera, the waterfall at frame center dropping past the dark cave mouth, mossy ochre walls, the pale river far below; the camera keeps a slow cinematic glide forward and down toward the falls, trailing the last shreds of cloud past the frame edges. 12–15s: the glide settles into a calm approach, mist drifting off the waterfall, the vista holding wide and clean. Audio: muffled rushing wind inside the cloud, opening up into wide open-air wind and a distant waterfall roar the moment the camera breaks through. No music.
```

### Fear-and-courage beat in cave before the jump — [AI vs VFX: Can Seedance 2.5 Beat VFX? (How To Make Realistic VFX Shots With Seedance 2.5)](ai-vs-vfx.md) P12
- Source: https://higgsfield.ai/blog/ai-vs-vfx | Model: Seedance 2.5 | Settings: one continuous handheld take ~14.5 s, dialogue

```text
SCENE CONTEXT
ONE UNBROKEN CONTINUOUS SHOT — a single take with zero cuts, inside the cave recess @cave_test directly behind a BIG living waterfall — and the falls are present in every frame even though they are never seen: a deep constant roar filling the cave, fine spray drifting in on the air, mist hanging in the bounce light, wet gleam on the gravel. A frontal medium shot on @adil_sheet_v2 — and this shot lives on his face: a young man alone in the dark, genuinely scared, wrestling his own fear out loud before jumping THROUGH that waterfall. He faces the camera — and the cliff edge with the water curtain behind it — backs away two-three unsteady steps deeper into the cave to build his run-up while talking himself into the jump, voice cracking braver than his eyes; a false start he aborts, a shaky laugh at himself, one last deep inhale-exhale — and he explodes into a sprint straight at the lens while the camera pushes forward to meet him, blowing past the frame edge and gone toward the water. True real-time speed, fully grounded and alive. Exactly one person in this shot; no dragon, no other people, no creatures.
ACTIVE REFERENCES
@cave_test: the location — 100% matches the reference. The cave recess behind the waterfall: dark textured basalt walls with horizontal strata, wet gleams and moss streaks, a loose gravel-and-rock floor darkened with damp near the mouth side, the interior receding into deep shadow. The bright cave mouth and the falling water curtain sit BEHIND the camera — never visible in frame, but physically present: their roar, their drifting spray, their cool moving air, and their soft bounce light all live inside the shot. Geography, materials, colors, and atmosphere follow the reference; no new landmarks are invented.
@adil_sheet_v2: Adil — HIGHEST IDENTITY PRIORITY, and there is EXACTLY ONE of him. His face is 100% locked to the reference in every frame: same bone structure, same dark brown eyes, same nose, same black mustache with a light chin patch, same short black curly hair and hairline, same small earrings in both ears, same warm skin tone, same beauty marks, same mid-20s age — the reference is the single source of truth, never regenerated, never averaged into a generic man, and EVERY emotional beat — the raw fear, the trembling breath, the nervous laugh, the set jaw of decision — is performed by this exact face. HIS EYES ARE ALIVE AND HUMAN: they blink naturally and irregularly throughout the take — quick nervous double-blinks, a hard blink after the down-glance, a slow half-second close on the reset — moist, catching light, never a frozen unblinking stare, never a dry mannequin gaze. Build: lean, average height. Wardrobe locked to the reference: a light-grey oversized nylon anorak hoodie with a half-zip, drawstring hood worn down, large front kangaroo flap pocket, and a small black curved wave logo embroidered on the left chest; a white t-shirt showing under the elastic hem; matching baggy light-grey nylon track pants with drawstring cuffs; chunky off-white trail sneakers; a thin silver chain at the neck — essentially dry, only a faint dusting of waterfall mist beading microscopically on the anorak shoulders and glinting in his curls, as anyone standing near big falls would carry. State: honestly frightened — heart hammering, hands unsteady — but forcing himself through it, the voice always half a step braver than the eyes.
LOCATION MAP
The camera stands inside the cave near the cliff edge, at Adil's chest height, aimed INTO the cave depth — its back to the unseen waterfall and daylight. Adil stands facing the camera a few meters in, on the gravel floor. Behind him: the cave runway — an open stretch of dark basalt floor receding into shadow, the far wall barely reading in the gloom. Between camera and Adil, and throughout the cave air: thin drifting waterfall mist, visible as a soft luminous haze in the bounce light, with occasional fine droplets floating through the frame and sparking tiny glints; the haze thins toward the cave depth. Soft bright bounce from the sky and water behind camera falls on his face and chest; the space behind him falls off into darkness. His movement axis is a straight line along the cave's length: first backward away from camera, deeper in — then forward at full sprint toward and past the camera, toward the edge and the falls behind it. The camera works on that same axis: easing backward as he retreats, then driving forward to meet his run. Camera stays on the axis the whole take; the waterfall is never shown.
FIRST FRAME AND SPATIAL BLOCKING
The first visible frame already contains Adil mid-emotion — no empty beat, no reveal, no neutral face: a frontal medium close-up, his face and shoulders filling the frame center, x 35% to 65%, and the fear already alive on him — eyes a little too wide aimed past the lens toward the edge, blinking fast, lips slightly parted around shallow breaths, one hand already rubbing its thumb across the fingertips; fine mist drifts through the light between him and the lens, a droplet glinting in his black curls; the pale grey anorak reads bright against the dark wet-gleaming cave wall behind him. Exactly one person in frame for the entire take. As he steps back the framing loosens from medium close-up toward a full medium shot — head to mid-thigh — always frontal, his face always toward camera, every flicker of it readable.
FORMAT MODE
ONE SINGLE UNBROKEN CONTINUOUS TAKE: zero cuts, zero transitions, zero frame jumps — one camera, one uninterrupted stream of real time at 1x natural speed.
OPTICS
47° diagonal field of view, standard normal lens character — natural human-eye perspective, zero distortion, natural face proportions, comfortable depth of field: Adil razor-sharp down to the catchlights and the mist beads, the cave wall behind him soft but textured. Occasional drifting droplets near the lens read as brief soft specks, never smears, never on-lens water. One lens, locked, no zoom, no focal change.
CAMERA
One camera, genuinely HANDHELD — a documentary-alive operator feel: constant subtle micro-shake, breathing sway, small weight shifts and human corrections, the horizon never dead level for long — organic and nervous like the scene itself, but never whip-panning, never lurching, never nauseating. One continuous three-phase behavior on the cave's long axis. Phase one, retreat with him: as Adil steps backward, the handheld camera walks backward too, mirroring his pace a half-beat behind with real footstep sway, the frame widening from medium close-up to medium. Phase two, settle: when he stops for the final breath, the operator plants — the shake calms to breathing-level micro-movement, sitting with him in the fear. Phase three, drive: the instant he launches, the camera pushes FORWARD toward him with real accelerating handheld energy — shake intensifying with the run — holding his face and torso as he grows huge in two-three strides; he blows past the camera's left edge with strong directional blur, and the camera carries its jolting momentum one more beat into the emptied cave before easing to a stop. No pan-whips, no zoom, no angle change — one axis, in and out.
ACTION TIMING
One continuous chain at true real-time speed — every second carries a living reaction; lips move ONLY for the scripted lines:
0.0s to 2.5s: first frame on his frightened face — fast shallow breathing lifting his shoulders, a quick nervous double-blink, eyes flicking once toward the drop past the lens and jerking back like the look burned; he takes one slow unsteady step backward, then another, sneakers feeling the damp gravel, hands opening and closing at his sides; a nervous wet lick of the lips; mist drifts through the frame; the handheld camera walks back with him.
2.5s to 4.5s: he stops half-step, eyes pinned past the lens, blinks hard once, and speaks quietly, clipped, the first words catching: "Okay… you can do this." — a hard visible swallow, throat bobbing above the white tee collar, and a tiny involuntary head-shake right after the line.
4.5s to 7.0s: one more small step back — the third — his eyes flick DOWN toward the unseen drop and his breath audibly hitches, shoulders flinching; he drags his gaze back up, blinks hard twice, and pushes the next line out: "You just have to jump." — the last word wobbling slightly.
7.0s to 8.5s: a FALSE START — he rocks forward onto his toes as if to go, gets half a lean into it, and bails: rocks back hard, huffs a shaky breath through puffed cheeks, and lets a tiny nervous laugh escape — one breathy "hh", a crooked half-smile flashing and dying — his hand raking back through his mist-damp black curls.
8.5s to 10.0s: he plants himself, jaw working, blinking fast, and says it again — steadier, angrier at his own fear: "Just run — and jump. That's it." — a small sharp nod sealing it.
10.0s to 11.5s: the reset — one long controlled exhale through pursed lips, the breath visibly shaking at its tail and faintly fogging in the cool waterfall air; he shakes both hands loose at the wrists, the loose anorak sleeves flapping once, bounces on his toes, rolls his neck; his eyes close in one slow half-second blink — and open locked past the camera, face still and set: decision beating fear by one point.
11.5s to 13.5s: he EXPLODES forward — first stride driving off the gravel, a raw sharp exhale-grunt tearing out on the launch, second and third strides accelerating hard, body lowering, arms pumping, the baggy nylon snapping around his limbs, teeth bared with effort, growing huge in frame as the handheld camera charges to meet him — and he blows past the camera's left edge in a rush of directional blur, gravel kicking, mist swirling in his wake.
13.5s to 14.5s: the camera's jolting momentum carries one more beat into the now-empty cave — dark runway, mist drifting back into stillness, his last footfalls and one fading breath echoing under the roar — end of the single take.
PHYSICS
Everything obeys real-world body mechanics at real rates, and the fear is fully physiological: shallow uneven chest rise, a hard swallow with visible throat movement, micro-tremor in the fingers and in the exhale, weight shifting foot to foot, ELEVATED NATURAL BLINK RATE — irregular, human, roughly every one-to-two seconds under stress, with the scripted hard blinks and the slow reset blink layered on top — a faint sheen of cold sweat and condensed mist on his temple and upper lip, the nervous lip-lick, the involuntary head-shake — all readable, never cartoonish, never a frozen stare. Loose-clothing physics: the oversized nylon anorak and baggy track pants move with real fabric behavior — rustling and shifting with every step, the kangaroo pocket flap bouncing lightly, the hood drawstrings swinging, the elastic hem riding over the white tee; in the sprint the slipstream plasters the loose fabric back against his chest and thighs while the sleeves and pant legs snap and ripple. The waterfall's presence is physical: cool air moves gently through the cave, drifting the mist in slow visible currents; fine droplets float, settle, and glint; his exhale fogs faintly; the gravel near the mouth side is dark with damp and grips differently underfoot — his launch strides bite it with a wet crunch. The backward steps are careful: heel feeling for the gravel, weight transferring fully, small arm corrections. The false start is honest biomechanics: weight loads forward, torso tips — the abort throws it back through the heels, arms flaring. The nervous laugh jolts his shoulders once; the hair-rake flicks a fine spray of mist off his curls. The sprint launch is earned: back-foot load, torso past base, short explosive first stride, lengthening acceleration, arms opposite legs, gravel scattering, one stone skittering. The pass-by is a real near-miss with a felt whoosh. Dust and mist hang and resettle after him. No floating, no rubbery limbs, no teleporting; the cave stays completely static.
LIGHTING
Natural motivated light only: soft bright bounce from the unseen sky and waterfall behind the camera keys Adil's face and chest — even, wrapping, gentle — while the cave falls into deep shadow behind him. The pale grey anorak catches the bounce and separates him cleanly against the dark basalt, the small black wave logo reading crisply on his left chest, the white tee glowing softly at the hem and collar. The drifting waterfall mist makes the light VISIBLE: a soft luminous haze between camera and subject, faint volumetric depth in the bounce, tiny droplet glints crossing the frame; wet basalt and damp gravel carry low specular gleams. Both eyes hold clear moving catchlights that flicker naturally with every blink; the too-wide eyes, the swallow, the down-flick, the dying half-smile, the sweat-and-mist sheen, the final set jaw — all fully lit and readable, no shadow ever crushing his features. As he retreats deeper the key softens one step and the haze thins; the sprint carries him back into the brighter, mistier zone, light rising on his face over the final strides. No sun disc, no rays, no lens flare, no artificial light. Exposure protects his face; the cave shadow keeps faint texture, never flat dead black.
AUDIO
One continuous soundscape, no breaks, and the WATERFALL OWNS THE ROOM: a deep, powerful, ever-present roar from behind the camera — low rumbling bass with a hissing spray top layer, swelling and easing in slow natural waves, echoing off the cave walls — loud enough that Adil speaks slightly over it; occasional fat droplets smack the gravel; the cool air moves audibly. Inside that: Adil's uneven breathing close and intimate, hitching at the down-glance; the soft nylon rustle of the anorak with every movement; damp gravel crunching under each backward step; his three quiet lines — "Okay… you can do this." … "You just have to jump." … "Just run — and jump. That's it." — spoken by Adil only, in English, exactly these words and nothing more, pushed slightly against the roar, the first delivery tight and thin, the last one firmer; between the second and third lines the huffed shaky breath and one tiny breathy laugh — non-word; the long trembling exhale of the reset; the soft slap of shaken hands; the raw sharp effort-grunt on the launch — non-word, Adil only; then explosive pounding footfalls rushing toward and past the lens with a whoosh, swallowed by the rising roar as he heads for the water. No other voices, no ad-libs, no music, no subtitles.
STYLE
Grounded photoreal cinema — intimate, tense, human; a fear-and-courage character beat against the physical presence of a giant waterfall. No 3D-render look, no game-engine smoothness, no slow motion. Natural 180-degree shutter motion blur at real-time speed: the dialogue and reaction beats hold razor-sharp through the handheld sway, the false start reads clean, the sprint and pass-by carry strong honest directional blur. Pore-level skin realism in the soft misty light — vellus hair, the adrenaline flush rising through the take, the cold-sweat-and-spray sheen, living blinking eyes with dancing catchlights. Real organic film grain, deep filmic contrast with lifted shadow detail. Muted grade: dominant dark basalt grays and umber, secondary soft daylight and mist on skin, the pale grey anorak and white tee as the light accents.
POSITIVE LOCKS
One single unbroken take: zero cuts, zero transitions, zero frame jumps, one handheld camera from first frame to last. Exactly one person in the entire shot — a single Adil, never duplicated; no dragon, no silhouettes, no figures in the shadows. Adil's identity locks to @adil_sheet_v2 with absolute priority in every frame — same face, dark brown eyes, black mustache with light chin patch, black curly hair, earrings, skin tone — and the same outfit: light-grey half-zip anorak hoodie with the black wave logo on the left chest, white tee underneath, baggy grey nylon track pants, off-white trail sneakers, thin silver chain — zero drift, zero substitution, no wardrobe changes, through every expression change; his face stays toward camera until the sprint carries him past it; the hood stays down the whole take. HIS EYES BLINK NATURALLY AND IRREGULARLY THE ENTIRE TAKE — nervous quick blinks, hard blinks, one slow reset blink — never more than two seconds without a blink, never a frozen stare. The waterfall's presence persists the whole take: constant roar, drifting visible mist, damp gravel, cool moving air — but the falls themselves are NEVER visible; they exist only as light, spray, and sound behind the camera. His clothes stay essentially dry with only faint mist beading — never soaked. The emotional arc locks in this exact order, each beat once: frightened breathing and fidgeting → line one with swallow and head-shake → third step back, down-glance with breath hitch → line two with a wobble → ONE false start aborted → ONE nervous breathy laugh with hair-rake → line three, firmer, with a nod → trembling fogged exhale reset with slow blink → explosive sprint with ONE effort-grunt → exit past the lens. Dialogue is ONLY the three scripted English lines in that order — no extra words, no ad-libs, no off-screen voices; the laugh and grunt are wordless. His fear is human-scale — trembling, hesitating, self-mocking — never sobbing, never screaming, never frozen panic. The camera moves only on the cave's long axis with organic handheld life: back with his retreat, planted for the breath, forward to meet his run — no pan-whips, no angle change, no zoom. Adil fully exits past the camera's left edge before the end, leaving at least one beat of empty misty cave. The whole shot plays at 1x real-time speed — no slow motion anywhere. 47° lens locked. Sharp clarity through natural handheld motion, stable exposure, no flickering, no ghosting.
```
