```text
ONE SINGLE CONTINUOUS UNBROKEN SHOT — one take, one camera, one uninterrupted recording, strictly real-time 1x speed: no editing, no slow motion, no freeze frames. Inside the cave recess behind the waterfall, facing out. Between the cliff lip and the falling water there is a wide corridor of open air — the waterfall hangs well away from the cave. The torrent itself is an immense, extremely dense mass of white falling water, flood-volume, so thick that nothing can be seen through it, throwing heavy spray. @adil_sheet_v2 is in the final stride of his sprint toward the edge; the camera chases behind him.
Camera — one continuous handheld chase from behind, 84° diagonal FOV, shoulder height, 2 m behind the runner. Hard endpoint: THE MOMENT HE LEAPS INTO THE VOID, THE CAMERA STOPS DEAD AT THE CLIFF LIP — no tilt down after him, no drift, no push in; from that instant it is a static locked frame to the last second. Lens locked:
First frame: his back mid-frame, full sprint, one step from the lip, gravel spraying off his heels, the torrent dead ahead. 0–1s: one last accelerating stride, camera shaking with the run. 1–2s: at the lip he dives off the cliff exactly like a diver entering water — arms swinging forward past his head, palms together, body stretching into one line and tipping over headfirst — screaming "AAAAAH!" as he goes over, dropping out of the bottom of the frame, the scream fading down; at this exact moment the camera stops dead at the lip and locks: the empty air corridor ahead, the water mass beyond it, drifting spray. 2–3s: ONE SECOND of stillness on that locked frame — no figure anywhere, only the falling water, drifting mist, the roar. Tension hangs in the emptiness. 3–5s: the dragon EXPLODES up into the frame from below the cliff edge — rising inside the air corridor, clearly in front of the torrent, closer to the lip than to the water: one violent vertical burst, sudden like a breaching whale, back and tail to camera, heavy directional motion blur, two brutal wing strokes, grit blasting over the lip — the rider pressed flat between the wing roots, jolting with the burst. 5–6s: at the top of the burst, in the same motion, it snaps over — head pitches forward, tail whips up — vertical momentum converting straight into forward drive, no hover. 6–15s: it charges FORWARD away from the camera and punches horizontally through the torrent: true fluid physics — tons of water shatter at the impact point, the dragon vanishing inside the mass for a split second, then bursting out the far side hauling sheets, ropes and spray on real ballistic arcs; water cascades off the wings; the torrent slams shut behind it as if never broken; fine droplets spatter the lens. It flies on into the bright haze, shrinking, the camera holding its locked frame to the end. Audio: footsteps and hard breathing, the raw scream fading into the roar, one second of waterfall thunder alone, then an eruption of wing-thunder from the gorge, the concussive detonation of the breached torrent, hissing away with distance. No music.
One unbroken take: camera moves only during the chase, stops dead at the leap, stays static to the last frame. He and the dragon are out of view only during the one-second stillness. Exactly one man and one dragon in the whole shot.
```

### Dragon bursts through waterfall veil, rider emotion beats — [AI vs VFX: Can Seedance 2.5 Beat VFX? (How To Make Realistic VFX Shots With Seedance 2.5)](ai-vs-vfx.md) P14
- Source: https://higgsfield.ai/blog/ai-vs-vfx | Model: Seedance 2.5 | Settings: one continuous take, 15 s, side-on aerial

```text
SCENE CONTEXT
ONE UNBROKEN CONTINUOUS SHOT — a single take with zero cuts, starting AT the waterfall in the moment of the burst: the shot opens on the thin waterfall veil of @waterfall_location_v1 seen strictly from the side, edge-on at screen-left — and within the first second @dragon_v1 with its single rider @adil_sheet_v2 explodes out through the water sheet and everything continues from there in one flow: the dragon gives one quick dog-like head-shake, its trailing fan of spray sweeps back and drenches Adil's face and hair in a natural shower of scattered droplets, he sputters a startled "oh-oh-oh!", clamps down in real fear of falling, steals a glance at the drop, risks a one-hand face-wipe and presses himself flatter — then a smile blooms and breaks into a raw, joyful adrenaline yell, he gives the dragon's neck two light grateful pats, and rides a tight exhilarated grin as the dragon accelerates, outruns the camera and exits frame-right. After the veil slides out of frame-left, NO waterfall ever appears again — not ahead, not in the background, not anywhere. Scale locked to @size_ref_v2. True real-time speed — no slow motion. Exactly one dragon and one rider; no other people, animals, or creatures anywhere.
ACTIVE REFERENCES
@waterfall_location_v1: the location — 100% matches the reference for geography, rock strata, materials, colors, and atmosphere: the basalt cliff, the light slender waterfall veil, the dark cave gap behind it, the gorge with its modest pool and outflow stream, canyon walls streaked ochre and green moss, hazy far ridges, flat overcast sky. The flow is a light veil, not a heavy column. In this shot the veil stands edge-on at screen-left only for the opening breach, then leaves frame forever. No new landmarks are invented; no second waterfall exists anywhere in this canyon.
@dragon_v1: the dragon — 100% matches the reference (anatomy, hide texture, wing membranes, spike rows, proportions). House-sized, charcoal-black, bat-like wings with translucent amber-brown membranes, rows of black spikes down the spine, long tapering spiked tail, four clawed limbs, hide like wet volcanic rock, streaming water; no saddle, no harness. It carries exactly one rider and no one else. Identity stays stable for the entire shot.
@size_ref_v2: the SIZE AND POSE REFERENCE — the scale law of this shot. Rider-to-dragon proportion locked exactly to this reference: the rider a small figure roughly one-tenth of the body length, seated at the neck base in front of the wings, legs astride the neck, dwarfed by the wingspan. The proportion never drifts in any frame.
@adil_sheet_v2: Adil, the rider — HIGHEST IDENTITY PRIORITY, and there is EXACTLY ONE of him: a single Adil at the neck base, never two riders, never a duplicate anywhere in frame. His face is 100% locked to the reference in every visible frame: same bone structure, same eyes, same nose, same thin mustache, same hairline and hair color, same skin tone, same mid-20s age — the reference is the single source of truth, never regenerated, never averaged into a generic man, never altered by water, blur, distance, or ANY expression: the shock, the fear, the grimace, the yell, and the grin are all performed by this exact face — expressions change, identity never does. Wardrobe locked: oversized light-grey hooded nylon anorak with a small black chest emblem over a white t-shirt, baggy light-grey nylon pants, off-white chunky sneakers, all soaked, hair plastered down keeping his exact hairline. He rides low against the neck base, arms wrapped around a neck spine. Zero identity drift from first visible frame to last.
LOCATION MAP
The camera sits OFFSET TO THE SIDE of the falls, not in front of them: its optical axis runs parallel to the cliff face, so the waterfall reads edge-on as a narrow translucent ribbon at screen-left — the front face of the falls is never visible and the falls are never frame-centered. Behind the veil, the dark cave gap from which the dragon erupts. The basalt cliff runs along frame-left and falls behind as the flight proceeds; the modest plunge pool and pale outflow stream sit at the veil's base, lower-left, visible only in the opening seconds. The right three-quarters of frame is open canyon air: layered hazy ridges, ochre and umber slopes, drifting thin mist, flat overcast sky. The flight line runs out of the cave perpendicular to the veil, then bends level and parallel to the cliff, screen-left to screen-right; the camera travels on a rail-straight lateral path on the canyon side of that line, holding the dragon in full side profile at constant height — the same side-on angle from first frame to last, never crossing the line, never rotating toward the falls.
FIRST FRAME AND SPATIAL BLOCKING
The first visible frame shows the veil edge-on at x 8% to 13%, falling from y 18% to y 80%, the dark cave gap behind it, mist and the small pool at its base lower-left — and the water is ALREADY reacting: the sheet visibly bulges outward around a dark mass pressing through from behind. This is a half-second live fuse, not a static establishing beat — the burst detonates within the first second. Once through, the frame holds exactly one dragon carrying exactly one rider at @size_ref_v2 scale: body spanning roughly x 25% to 65%, wingspan about 60% of frame width, Adil a small soaked figure about 4% of frame height at the neck base, seated on the camera side of the neck, head in three-quarter view toward camera — every expression readable for the entire take, never hidden behind the neck. The dragon's back behind him and the tail are completely empty.
FORMAT MODE
ONE SINGLE UNBROKEN CONTINUOUS TAKE: zero cuts, zero transitions, zero fades, zero frame jumps — one camera, one uninterrupted stream of real time at 1x natural speed.
OPTICS
84° diagonal field of view, classic wide-angle lens character. The full side-on wingspan fits in frame with air around it, straight basalt strata stay rectilinear, deep focus from the near wing to the far ridge, no fisheye curve — Adil's face and every micro-expression hold sharp at the flight distance. Stray droplets near the lens read as brief soft specks, never smears. One lens, locked, no zoom, no focal change.
CAMERA
One aerial camera, hovering side-on at mid-veil height as the shot opens, framing the bulging water sheet at screen-left. At the burst it picks up the dragon and eases into a lateral tracking move — lagging half a beat with heavy-platform inertia, then matching the dragon's speed on a rail-straight path parallel to the flight line, holding it centered in full side profile at constant distance and constant height — the exact same side-on angle for the entire take. The waterfall edge slides out of frame-left within the first two seconds and the frame never turns back toward it. In the final phase the dragon accelerates beyond the camera's pace: the camera eases its ramp and settles, the dragon pulls ahead, shrinks slightly with true distance, and exits frame-right. Tilt and roll stay fixed; every speed change is smooth and weighted. No pan-whips, no zoom, no push-in, no reframing jolts, no crossing the action line.
ACTION TIMING
One continuous chain of cause and effect at true real-time speed — Adil's face carries a readable emotional beat in every phase:
0.0s to 0.8s: the veil bulges — the dark mass pushes the sheet visibly outward, water tenting around the shape of a skull.
0.8s to 2.2s: THE BURST — the veil parts around the skull with almost no resistance, ribbons whipping aside and collapsing into spray, water sheeting off brow and shoulders, wings tearing free and snapping open, the last streamers shredding into fine droplets as the whole body clears the water and levels into flight, moving screen-left to screen-right; Adil rides it adrenaline-wide — eyes shining, mouth half-open gulping wind, knuckles white on the spine, a tense thrilled almost-smile flickering.
2.2s to 3.4s: ONE quick head-shake — a fast dog-like rotary shake of skull and upper neck, two or three oscillations in under a second, water leaving tangentially in helical arcs that break into fans of separate droplets — and the trailing fan sweeps back along the slipstream and rakes over Adil as a natural drenching shower: scattered droplets pepper his face and hair, fresh water streaming down his cheeks and off his chin, his wet curls flattening harder against his forehead; he flinches and blinks into it, head ducking a fraction — a soaking, not an impact.
3.4s to 5.2s: shock beat — showered and dripping, he sputters, spits a mouthful of water sideways, jerks droplets off his own head, blurts a breathless startled "oh-oh-oh!", ducks and clamps his whole body flat against the neck, both arms cinching around the spine, face scrunched, eyes squeezed shut.
5.2s to 7.5s: fear beat — still pressed low he cracks one eye open, steals a wide-eyed glance DOWN at the canyon drop, visibly swallows hard, jaw tightening — genuinely afraid of falling; then the risky wipe: knees and one arm clamp harder as he peels the other hand off the spine, drags the heel of his palm down across his face in one firm wipe with a squinting grimace, clearing his eyes and slicking wet curls off his forehead, and immediately slaps the hand back onto the spine — pressing himself flatter than before, chest to hide, cheek almost touching it.
7.5s to 9.5s: joy breaks through — vision clear, still gripping, he puffs out a huge cheek-swelling exhale, and a smile spreads across the dripping face, wider and wider, until he throws his head into the wind and lets out a raw, joyful adrenaline yell — half laugh, half scream, a full unhurried breath of it, veins standing in his neck, water flying off his hair — pure pleasure, zero fear left in it.
9.5s to 11.0s: gratitude beat — steady level flight, the yell settling into ragged happy breaths; Adil clamps down with knees and one arm, lifts the other hand and gives the dragon's neck hide two light, affectionate open-palm pats — a small "thank you", each contact clearly readable — then plants the hand straight back onto the spine.
11.0s to 13.5s: exhilaration and acceleration — the dragon stretches its head low and surges, wingbeats quickening from one every two seconds toward one per second; Adil's grin narrows into a tight, wind-slitted exhilarated grin, eyes bright against the airflow, body glued low but riding the surge now instead of hiding from it; background parallax speeds up continuously; the frame is pure open canyon — no waterfall anywhere; the camera eases off its ramp and holds.
13.5s to 15.0s: the dragon pulls ahead and exits frame-right completely, tail last, Adil's grin the last readable human detail; the locked side-on frame holds a full final beat on empty canyon air — drifting mist, hazy ridges, no waterfall — end of the single take.
PHYSICS
Everything obeys real-world physics at real-world rates, at true house-sized @size_ref_v2 scale — heavy wingbeats, huge inertia, nothing flutters. The burst: the dragon's mass drives a pressure bow-wave that tents the sheet before contact; a light curtain parts around mass rather than exploding — ribbons whip aside, cling briefly to the wet hide, slide off in sheets along the body's curves, then drop away; heavy drops fly farthest in clean parabolas, fine mist stalls and hangs; the parted veil re-threads only from the lip downward as new water arrives, sliding out of frame as it does. The head-shake obeys rotation physics: the skull oscillates fast around the neck axis, loose skin lagging behind bone, water leaving tangentially in helical sheets that immediately break into chains and fans of separate droplets — no ropes, no jets, no single connected mass; the aft-swept fan is carried by the slipstream and rakes across the rider as distinct drops of many sizes, each on its own ballistic arc, peppering his face and hair, running off his chin, secondary droplets ripped away by the wind; the neck damps the shake in two beats and the flight line never wavers. Adil's reactions are body-mechanical and cost real effort: the sputter sprays fine droplets ripped away by the slipstream; his head-jerk flings water from his hair in a small fan; the flinch is whole-body — shoulders jump, grip cinches with visible arm strain; the downward glance freezes him for a beat, throat moving in the swallow; the one-handed wipe is a genuine risk, so knees and the remaining arm clamp visibly harder while the free hand works, the palm dragging the water off in a smeared sheet torn away instantly by the wind; the hand plants back and the torso flattens further; the yell shakes his chest, breath tearing away visibly in the airstream; the neck pats are the same disciplined mechanics — clamp first, brief one-hand release, two soft palm contacts that knock a thin film of water off the wet hide in small splashes, hand straight back to the spine. The acceleration is earned stroke by stroke: wing spars bend visibly under each downstroke, membranes belly and snap flat with tension wrinkles then slacken a fraction on the upstroke, cadence rises, glide gaps shorten, the neck stretches into a drag-tight line, the spiked tail streams straight with whip-lag — background parallax grows continuously, plus true perspective shrink as the dragon pulls away from the settled camera. Water on the hide sheds heaviest in the burst and the shake, thinning to a fine trailing spray; wet hide gleams, drying patches go matte. Adil's soaked anorak stays pinned flat by the slipstream, its loose hem snapping faster as speed climbs; nothing distorts, covers, or reshapes his facial features — every expression lives on the exact reference face. Mist drifts on one consistent wind. No floating, no rubbery motion, no teleporting; cliffs and canyon stay completely static.
LIGHTING
Flat overcast daylight — the cloud dome as one giant soft source from above; no sun disc, no rays, no cast shadows, no lens flare, light constant for the whole shot. Soft even sky light keeps every beat of Adil's face fully readable: the adrenaline-wide eyes with bright catchlights at the burst, the droplet shower glistening on his cheeks and curls at the shake, the scrunched shut-eyed flinch, the one-eyed downward glance, the grimace of the wipe, the blooming smile, the open-mouthed joyful yell, the two gentle pats, and the final narrowed grin — the thin mustache resolved throughout, no shadow ever crushing his features. The burst ribbons and the head-shake droplet fans flash bright against the dark cliff; the veil reads semi-translucent against the cave gap while in frame. The wet charcoal hide reads by broad soft speculars; the amber-brown membranes glow faintly translucent on each upstroke — the single warm color accent of the frame. Aerial haze steps the far ridges brighter and cooler with distance. Exposure protects the white water, the spray, and Adil's face — highlights keep texture, never clip, gentle halation on the burst.
AUDIO
One continuous soundscape, no breaks: the waterfall's hiss and a swelling pressure surge in the first half-second; the wet tearing splash of the burst at 0.8s; wings snapping open; the hiss fading out left by 2.2s; at 2.3s the rapid wet flutter-slap of the head-shake and the raking hiss of the droplet fan sweeping over the rider; at 3.5s Adil's sputter and spit, then his quick startled "oh-oh-oh!"; a tight audible swallow as he glances down; the squeak-drag of a wet palm across skin at 6.5s; at 8s one big whooshing exhale rolling straight into a raw, joyful adrenaline yell — half laugh, half scream, torn by the wind, riding a full breath; at 10s two soft wet pats of a palm on hide in the steady wingbeat rhythm — Adil's only vocalizations: the sputter, the "oh-oh-oh!", and the joyful yell, no words, no other voices; then wingbeats coming quicker and quicker, wind noise climbing, the whole mass of sound doppler-fading right as the dragon pulls away; open canyon wind alone on the empty end frame. No music, no subtitles.
STYLE
Epic photoreal cinema — grounded physical realism, no 3D-render look, no game-engine smoothness, no slow-motion aesthetics. Natural 180-degree shutter motion blur at real-time speed: the burst, the head-shake, and the downstrokes carry strong directional blur on water and wings, resolving into sharp readable beats for every one of Adil's expressions — the shower, the sputter, the glance, the wipe, the smile, the yell, the pats, the grin — blur never smears his features into a different face. Real organic film grain, deep filmic contrast curve with lifted shadow detail. Muted desaturated grade — dominant ochre and umber terrain, secondary cool gray sky and haze, white water accent — with Adil's emotional arc as the heart of the frame.
POSITIVE LOCKS
One single unbroken take: zero cuts, zero transitions, zero frame jumps, one camera from first frame to last. The shot starts AT the waterfall and ON the burst: the veil is already bulging in frame one and the dragon breaks through within the first second — no long empty establishing beat, no cave interior, no approach shot. The waterfall appears ONLY edge-on at screen-left during the burst, is never frame-centered, exits frame-left by 2.2s, and NO waterfall ever appears again anywhere in the frame — no second falls, no water curtain in the background, none. The camera holds one strict side-on lateral angle the entire time — optical axis parallel to the cliff, full profile on the dragon, constant height — no push-in, no pan-whip, no angle change, no crossing the line. Adil's emotional arc locks in this exact order and only once each: adrenaline-wide eyes at the burst → ONE head-shake (2.2s–3.4s) → ONE natural drenching droplet shower over his face and hair — the spray reaches him only as scattered separate droplets on ballistic arcs, always a shower, never a rope, jet or slap → sputter and spit → startled "oh-oh-oh!" and full-body clamp → ONE scared glance down with a swallow → ONE face-wipe with the heel of his hand while gripping harder → press flatter → big exhale blooming into a smile → ONE raw joyful adrenaline yell → TWO light one-hand pats on the neck with the other arm and knees clamped, hand straight back to the spine → tight exhilarated grin to the exit. His vocalizations are only the sputter, the "oh-oh-oh!", and the joyful yell — no words ever; his fear is small and human — afraid of falling, gripping harder — and the only scream in the shot is the yell of joy. After the pats the dragon only accelerates — speed never drops — and it fully exits frame-right before the end, leaving at least one full beat of empty frame. Adil's face locks to @adil_sheet_v2 with absolute priority in every visible frame — same eyes, nose, thin mustache, hairline, skin tone — zero drift, zero substitution, through every expression change; his face stays in three-quarter view toward camera whenever visible. Exactly one rider total — a single Adil, never duplicated; the dragon's back and tail stay empty. Exactly one dragon, 100% matching @dragon_v1, zero identity drift. Scale locked to @size_ref_v2 — the rider one-tenth of the body length, never drifting. The whole shot plays at 1x real-time speed — no slow motion anywhere. Screen direction locks left to right. No other creatures, people, vehicles, or buildings ever appear. Overcast holds; no sun ever appears. 84° lens locked. Sharp clarity, stable picture, no flickering, no ghosting.
```

### High-speed gorge chase with lens droplets — [AI vs VFX: Can Seedance 2.5 Beat VFX? (How To Make Realistic VFX Shots With Seedance 2.5)](ai-vs-vfx.md) P15
- Source: https://higgsfield.ai/blog/ai-vs-vfx | Model: Seedance 2.5 | Settings: one continuous shot, 21:9, chase drone

```text
SCENE CONTEXT
A colossal dragon with its rider races low through a winding rocky gorge, weaving around jutting rock buttresses and punching through a low-hanging cloud shred while a hard-shaken chase camera follows behind its tail, its lens catching spray; bursting into an open reach, the camera arcs 180° around the flying dragon and ends dead ahead, facing it head-on. One continuous chase-to-reveal shot, real time.
ACTIVE REFERENCES
@dragon_v1 — the dragon: charcoal-black hide like wet volcanic rock, translucent amber-brown wing membranes, black dorsal spike rows, long spiked tail, wingspan of fourteen men laid head to toe, heavy as five elephants. No saddle, no harness. 100% matches the reference; ignore the sheet's grey background.
@adil_sheet_v2 — the rider: mid-20s man, short dark curly hair, damp and wind-whipped, thin black mustache, damp light-grey hooded anorak with a small black curled chest emblem, bare hands. 100% matches the reference; ignore the sheet layout and studio background.
@size_ref_v2 — proportion and mount only: the rider sits astride the neck base between the wing roots at exactly this rider-to-dragon scale, held in every frame; his face and wardrobe come from @adil_sheet_v2, the man in this reference is a scale stand-in. Ignore its background and lighting.
LOCATION MAP
A wild, winding Icelandic gorge under a heavy broken cloud deck. Foreground: braided shallows of a pale glacial river threading between moss-capped boulders the size of houses. Midground: the gorge bends left then right, fractured basalt buttresses and rock spurs jutting alternately from either wall into the flight path, scree fans at their feet, gaps between spur and wall narrower than the dragon's wingspan; one ragged shred of low cloud hangs inside the gorge below the rim, snagged across the flight line. Background: broken stepped walls streaked ochre and green moss rising to a ragged skyline, dense grey-white cumulus stacked above with narrow torn gaps, one thin sun blade sweeping the far wall, cloud shadows crawling over the rock.
FIRST FRAME / BLOCKING
Chase framing from directly behind: the dragon fills the mid-frame flying away from camera at 130 km/h, three meters above the braided river, wings at full span, @adil_sheet_v2 leaning low on its neck base between the wing roots, both hands locked on a dorsal spike; the first bend of the gorge and its jutting buttress stand ahead, half in soft cloud light, the low cloud shred visible beyond; the frame already carries a fine speed vibration and two small water beads at its lower edge.
FORMAT MODE
One continuous shot, real time throughout; the camera does not cut on its own.
OPTICS
84° diagonal FOV, rectilinear, one wide prime character for the whole shot, natural 180° shutter motion blur, focal length locked; a physical front element riding open in the airstream — water beads and streaks live on the lens glass and refract the scene like a hard-mounted action camera.
CAMERA
Chase drone 15 meters behind and slightly right of the tail at matched 130 km/h, three meters altitude: through the gorge it banks with every turn a half-beat late, threads the same gaps, and punches through the same cloud shred two beats after the dragon. At 10 seconds, over the open reach, it accelerates hard, swings wide out to the dragon's right flank and carves a level 180° arc around it — past the full side profile at 12 seconds — settling dead ahead of the dragon at 13 seconds, flying backward at matched speed, aimed straight down its incoming flight line, holding constant distance for the final beats. The whole chase rides a constant fine speed vibration — a one-to-two-centimeter high-frequency tremor — with discrete jolts on top: a kick in every wingbeat wash, a hard buffet through the cloud shred, and continuous heavy buffeting inside the dragon's bow-wave through the backward-flying head-on finale. The arc's path itself stays smooth and machine-driven; the shake lives on top of it as fine vibration, never swinging the framing off the subject. Focus rides the dragon and rider throughout.
ACTION
0–4s: full-speed run up the braided river — each heavy downstroke slams a pressure ring onto the water and rips twin spray vortices off the wingtips, a V-shaped wake tearing between the boulders; the first fine spray beads tick onto the lens glass and streak toward the frame edges in the airstream; the dragon lifts a wing to clear a house-sized moss-capped monolith by arm-lengths; the rider flattened low, anorak cracking. 4–7s: the first bend — the dragon rolls into a knife-edge left bank around a jutting buttress, wings near-vertical so the span fits between spur and wall, the low wingtip slicing a curtain of spray that rakes across the lens — a burst of distinct droplets lands and stays, beads trembling with the vibration, crawling outward; mid-move the buttress whips between camera and dragon, wiping the frame for two beats. 7–10s: out of the bank straight into the hanging cloud shred — the dragon punches through it, vapor exploding off its leading edges and streaming from the wingtips in torn ribbons; the camera punches through two beats later with a hard buffet — a wet film fogs the glass for a breath and tears away in the airstream, leaving fresh beads scattered across the frame — and they burst out the far side together into an opening, wider reach of the gorge. 10–13s: the dragon holds straight, level, powerful flight up the middle of the open reach, vapor still shedding off its spikes, while the camera arcs around it; as the thin sun blade through a cloud gap crosses the flight line the wing membranes flash translucent amber against the dark walls, and every bead on the lens glass flares into a tiny prismatic spark. 13–15s: head-on at last — the dragon barrels straight at the lens in full frontal power, head low, wingbeats hammering toward camera, wing-thrown spray spattering one last fresh burst across the glass, beads shivering in the bow-wave buffet; @adil_sheet_v2 stays low over the neck, both hands still welded to the spike, and plays the whole beat with his face alone — the grin cracking wide into a shredded yell of pure joy, brows high, eyes blazing straight past the lens between the trembling droplets. Displaced air shoves mist outward on every pass.
PERFORMANCE
Both of the rider's hands grip the neck spike in every single frame — they never open, never lift, never wave. Everything lives in the body lean and the face: pressed flat through the banks, head tucked behind the spike in the cloud punch, then in the head-on finale the face carries it all — grin breaking into a yell, cheeks shuddering in the airstream, damp hair whipping, catch-lights flaring as the sun blade passes.
PHYSICS
Thirty tons at 130 km/h: carved banks with visible wing flex, spray and wake obeying the wingbeat rhythm, vapor tearing honestly around the body in the cloud punch, real inertia in every turn; through the camera's arc the dragon's own flight stays straight and level — all curving motion belongs to the camera. Droplets on the glass obey the airstream — landing as distinct beads, trembling with the vibration, streaking toward the frame edges; the wet film from the cloud punch tears off in under a second. The rider's 80 kg leans a fraction ahead of every roll. Gaps narrower than the wingspan are passed only in knife-edge bank or half-fold, wingtips clearing rock by arm-lengths.
LIGHTING
Heavy broken-cloud daylight: dominant soft luminous 6000K cloud light with crawling shadow patches, one hard 5600K sun blade sweeping through a torn gap — crossing the flight line exactly at the camera arc's side point, flaring the membranes amber contre-jour and sparking the lens beads; wet hide and churned water picking soft speculars, cool haze deepening down-gorge, the cloud shred glowing from within as they punch it.
AUDIO
Wind and water only: hammering wingbeats with spray hiss, the ripping rush of the wake, wet spray ticking and spattering against the camera housing, a doppler whoosh as the buttress whips past the lens, a muffled white roar inside the cloud shred, the building thunder and buffet rumble of the head-on approach, the rider's shredded yell. No music, no subtitles.
STYLE
Photoreal live-action aerial chase cinematography, 8K-level detail with fine grain, true reptilian anatomy, real water, vapor and fabric behavior, crisp Icelandic grade — cool shadows, rare warm sun accents.
OUTPUT SETTINGS
Real time start to finish, one uninterrupted take, 21:9.
POSITIVE LOCKS
Exactly one dragon and one rider; the rider stays seated astride the neck base in every frame at the @size_ref_v2 proportion, both hands gripping the neck spike from first frame to last, one continuous anatomically intact silhouette from every viewing angle of the arc — identity and wardrobe identical from behind, from the side and head-on. The shake stays a fine constant vibration and the lens droplets refract without hiding the action — the dragon and the rider's face stay clearly readable in every frame. Wings, rock and camera never intersect or touch; the 180° arc happens over the open reach, clear of all rock. Damp state and wardrobe identical from first frame to last.
```

### Rise out of cloud deck into chase — [AI vs VFX: Can Seedance 2.5 Beat VFX? (How To Make Realistic VFX Shots With Seedance 2.5)](ai-vs-vfx.md) P16
- Source: https://higgsfield.ai/blog/ai-vs-vfx | Model: Seedance 2.5 | Settings: global header + CUT 1

```text
Style: 8K IMAX. Photorealistic — no 3D render, no game engine.
Lighting: Natural light only — contre-jour backlight, camera on shadow side, atmospheric haze throughout. Key light from the sky only. No artificial lighting.
Color: 60:30:10 — dominant / secondary / accent.
Camera: Physical cine lens. 180° shutter motion blur.
Skin: Pore-level realism — vellus hair, asymmetric moles, capillary flush, pore-shadow matching on-set light.
Acting: Hollywood — micro-pauses before reactions, precise eye-line, living eyes with catch-lights, chest rise from breathing. Characters never standing, always reacting.
Physics: Gravity and inertia respected — mass has real weight, correct contact shadows. No floating props.
Composition: Rule of thirds + golden ratio.
Continuity: Characters, props, environment identical across every cut. No identity drift.
Technical: 24fps smooth motion. 8K detail. No jitter.
Audio: Environmental SFX only. No music. No subtitles.
Characters:
@dragon_v1 — the dragon, 100% matches the @dragon_v1 reference (anatomy, hide texture, wing membranes, spike rows): charcoal-black dragon, bat-like translucent amber-brown membrane wings, long spiked tail.
@adil_sheet_v2 — the rider, identity and wardrobe 100% match the @adil_sheet_v2 reference: mid-20s man, short dark curly hair, thin black mustache; oversized light-grey hooded nylon anorak with a small black chest emblem over a white t-shirt, baggy light-grey nylon pants, off-white chunky sneakers, fabric still damp from the falls.
@size_ref_v2 — controls only the rider-to-dragon size ratio and the seating: the rider sits astride the base of the dragon's neck where it meets the shoulders, between the wing roots, legs hanging along the dragon's flanks, hands on the neck ridge in front of him — scale and seat position exactly as in @size_ref_v2; his upper body may lean with the flight (pressed low at speed and through the dives, tall in calm glides) but the seat position and the size ratio never change. The reference's own background, light, camera and the rider's face are not inherited. Exactly one dragon and one rider.
Scene:
The interior and sunlit top of an endless cloud deck. The shot begins deep inside the cloud: cold, dense, bright-grey vapor in every direction, completely empty — no figure, no shadow, nothing but fog. Above the deck: gold-white cloud tops stretching to the horizon under a deep blue-white sky, a low golden sun dead ahead on the flight line. The world below the clouds is never visible. The camera climbs alone up through the empty cloud interior, breaks out above the tops — and only then the dragon and its rider become visible, already flying ahead on the sun line, freshly surfaced themselves; the camera settles into pursuit behind them.
CUT 1 — Continuous solo rise-and-breakout into a low forward chase from behind, 84° diagonal FOV, camera climbing steadily through the empty cloud interior, punching out above the tops, tipping level onto the sun line, finding the dragon ahead and settling into a steady tail-on pursuit, easing off at the end, lens locked:
First frame: full whiteout deep inside the cloud — cold, dense, bright-grey vapor filling the frame edge to edge, luminous but directionless, thick strands sliding past with the camera's climb; nothing else exists in the fog. 0–2s: the empty climb — the camera rises alone through churning cold-grey density, the murk packed and featureless, brightness slowly building overhead as the top nears, thin streaks of paler light beginning to lance down through the thinning vapor; no silhouette, no shadow, only fog and wind. 2–3s: the breakout — the camera punches out of the deck into open air and tips level: the golden cloud sea unrolls to the horizon, the sun disc low and dead ahead — and there they are, revealed in the same instant: @dragon_v1 two body-lengths ahead on the sun line, tail-on to camera, wings hauling in heavy beats, @adil_sheet_v2 pressed low at the neck base, ribbons of vapor still pouring off the wings, tail and flanks and a slowly closing swirl in the veil beneath them — both freshly surfaced from the same deck. 3–5s: the camera settles into tail-on pursuit as the pair levels into flight just above the breathing tops, the dragon's downwash rolling waves of mist in its wake, the last vapor shedding off its spikes; the rider straightens tall in the seat for a beat, taking in the golden sea. 5–6.5s: the dragon tips nose-down, folds its wings half-closed and knifes back into the cloud deck ahead — the mist swallows it in one soft upwelling swirl that curls closed behind its tail, no splash, the rider pressing low into the dive; for a beat only its dark bulk races on as a shadow beneath the thin veil, plowing a stirred furrow of glowing mist that trails and slowly smooths over — the camera holds its line above the tops. 6.5–9s: the shadow surges and the dragon bursts out in a long porpoising leap — a higher, slower arc, wings snapping open and shedding ribbons of fog that evaporate mid-air, the rider flat against the neck, sunlight flashing through the amber membranes at the peak — then it folds and knifes back under a second time, the deck swallowing it in another soft swirl, the running shadow and furrow racing on toward the sun. 9–11s: the final eruption — the dragon climbs out for good in a steep rising arc, vapor pouring off the membranes and the long spiked tail in soft dissolving sheets, and levels into flight just above the tops, its downwash rolling waves of mist in its wake, the camera holding pursuit low enough that stray wisps whip past the lens edges. 11–13.5s: the silhouette slides onto the low sun disc — the frame dips into deep amber, the wing membranes flare translucent fire, a soft lens flare wraps the black silhouette, and the near-eclipse holds through two slow wingbeats. 13.5–15s: the dragon glides off the far edge of the disc, the exposure settles, and dragon and rider melt into the golden glare over the cloud sea as the camera eases off, last wisps drifting past the lens. Audio: a cold muffled cocoon at the start — thin interior hiss and wind only, no wingbeats, nothing alive in the fog — the wind rising and brightening with the climb, a burst into wide-open high-altitude wind at the breakout joined at once by the heavy wingbeats of the dragon ahead, calm powerful strokes over the tops, a deep whoosh and a racing muffled hiss through the dive and shadow-run, a huge vapor-shredding whoosh at the leap and the final eruption, building airspeed roar through the low run, then the sound thinning to near-silence through the held eclipse, ending on wide-open wind. No music. Natural 180-degree shutter motion cadence: real motion blur in the murk, the shredding mist, the dives and the leaps, sharp readable silhouette at the reveal, at each arc peak and against the sun disc.
Positive constraints: one continuous unbroken camera move, no cuts; inside the cloud the frame holds nothing but cold, dense, bright-grey vapor — no dragon, no rider, no silhouette, no shadow, no wingbeat sound until the breakout; the interior vapor is cold and luminous grey, never dark, and the light only ever brightens and warms toward the top, never cools back; the dragon and rider become visible for the first time in the instant the camera clears the deck — already ahead of it, tail-on, freshly surfaced, trailing vapor; the camera surfaces exactly once and never re-enters the deck; the cloud behaves as weightless vapor throughout — it parts, swirls, shreds and slowly refills, and never ripples, splashes or tears like water, jelly or fabric; after the reveal the dragon dives beneath the deck exactly twice and emerges exactly twice more, in clean porpoising arcs, always ahead of the camera; the camera never overtakes it; the dragon crosses the sun disc exactly once, at the end; exactly one dragon and one rider appear in the whole shot.
```

```text
Cinematic 15-second continuous sequence, 16:9, i2v. Character: the young man from the character reference, beige brushed-wool zip bomber jacket, grey turtleneck under white tee, dark indigo wide-leg jeans, brown loafers, slicked-back blond hair. Location: the New York SoHo street from the location reference, cast-iron facades, fire escapes, parked yellow cabs, hazy backlit sky down the avenue. 0-3s: Wide static shot, low tripod height, 35mm. He steps onto the zebra crosswalk and takes exactly two slow steps, then stops mid-stride as a deep mechanical hum builds overhead, an unfamiliar low-frequency drone layered over distant police sirens. He freezes, listening, weight still on the front foot. 3-6s: Cut to medium shot, chest-up, 50mm, shallow depth of field, street softly blurred behind him. Still standing in place, he turns his head sharply left, then right, then tilts it slightly as the humming grows, searching for the source of the sound. Jaw tightening, eyes narrowing. Sirens rise and echo off the facades. Micro handheld drift. 6-10s: Cut to extreme low angle from ground level, camera tilting up past his shoulder. A colossal dark metallic spaceship glides slowly along the length of the street directly overhead, moving away from camera deeper down the avenue, filling the frame, panel-lined underbelly with pulsing blue running lights, casting a huge moving shadow that sweeps over him and the parked cars. He tilts his head back and stares up, lips parting in awe. Deep sub-bass rumble overwhelming the sirens, dust and paper debris lifting off the asphalt, volumetric light rays breaking around the hull. 10-13s: Cut to over-the-shoulder shot from directly behind him, his back and beige jacket filling the left third of frame, slightly out of focus. Camera looks down the avenue in the same direction the spaceship is heading. Three police cars tear past him one after another, driving away from camera down the street, chasing after the ship in the same direction it flies, roof lightbars strobing red and blue across the asphalt and his jacket, Doppler sirens ripping past. He stays planted, head turning to follow them. Gentle forward gimbal push, motion blur on the vehicles, red-blue taillight glow receding into the haze. 13-15s: Cut to final wide static shot. He stands alone and small in the middle of the empty crosswalk, back to camera, watching the spaceship and the police lights shrink together into the hazy skyline at the end of the street. Steam drifting from a manhole, distant fading sirens, low ominous drone. Slow subtle push-in. Backlit natural daylight, teal-and-amber cinematic grade, atmospheric haze, film grain, 35mm/50mm lenses, shallow depth of field, consistent character wardrobe and face throughout. No text, no captions, no watermarks.
```

### Locker-room girl one-take stare (sectioned prompt with locks) — [Kling 3.0 on Higgsfield: A Guide to the Next Era of AI Video Generation](Kling-3.0-is-on-Higgsfield-User-Guide-AI-Video-Generation.md) P3
- Source: https://higgsfield.ai/blog/Kling-3.0-is-on-Higgsfield-User-Guide-AI-Video-Generation | Model: Kling 3.0 | Settings: 6 s one take, @GIRL + @LOC references, handheld push-in, no music

```text
SCENE CONTEXT Six-second one-take: a red-haired girl in a navy tracksuit on the floor of a deep-red locker room: jacket-fix, room-scan, then a level stare into the lens as the handheld camera pushes in to a close portrait.
ACTIVE REFERENCES @GIRL: 100% per reference: copper-red hair pulled back, loose strands; steel-blue eyes; navy track jacket, white chest panel, navy pants, dark-red sneakers. Seated, one knee up. Contained defiance: fix as armor-adjustment, scan as a fighter reading exits, the stare a statement. @LOC: per reference: deep-RED locker room: crimson panels, red lockers with chrome latches, white tile, one cool overhead pool into red gloom.
FORMAT / CAMERA ONE UNBROKEN TAKE, 6.0s, zero cuts. First frame = reference composition, already alive: hands on the hem, eyes down, no empty start. 47 degrees natural, rectilinear, focus locked on her face. MAXIMUM HANDHELD: breath-sway, micro-corrections, framing by feet, never zoom, never stabilized. ONE continuous move: slow walking push 2.5m to 1.2m, waist-up to close portrait, at its closest exactly as her eyes land on the lens.
ACTION TIMING 0-2.5s, THE FIX. Head down. Both hands tug the hem, squaring the white panel; collar flick, a copper strand falls across her temple; she leaves it. The zipper stays untouched, fingers never near it. Camera breathes, then advances on the collar flick. 2.5-4.5s, THE SCAN. Hands settle on the knee. Eyes sweep LEFT into the red depth, hold, track RIGHT along the chrome latches: reading the room, not the lens. Camera closing. 4.5-6.0s, THE LOOK. Eyes come off the lockers, land level on the lens. Chin lifts a degree. One slow blink, then stillness, breath in the shoulders. Camera settles into close portrait, holds. Ends MID-STARE.
PHYSICS / LIGHT / AUDIO Real breath, true blink timing, nylon rustle, the strand staying. One cool overhead key carves cheekbones and the white panel; red gloom, red bounce, chrome speculars; her face takes more key as the camera nears. Ventilation hum, fabric rustle, one nose-exhale. No music, no dialogue.
POSITIVE LOCKS Order locked: fix (zipper untouched), scan left-right, eyes land on the lens and HOLD. Eyes meet the lens exactly ONCE, at the end. ONE camera move. ONE person; 100% per reference. 8K photoreal, 180 degree shutter, pore-level skin, true fabric/hair physics; no 3D render, no plastic skin. NO IP / NO BRANDS / NO LOGO
```

### Macro push-in on vintage field watch — [Kling 3.0 on Higgsfield: A Guide to the Next Era of AI Video Generation](Kling-3.0-is-on-Higgsfield-User-Guide-AI-Video-Generation.md) P4
- Source: https://higgsfield.ai/blog/Kling-3.0-is-on-Higgsfield-User-Guide-AI-Video-Generation | Model: Kling 3.0 | Settings: single continuous macro dolly-in, 6 s, audio ticking

```text
SCENE CONTEXT A single continuous extreme macro push-in on a scratched vintage military field watch held in a man's fingers, from a full view of the dial into an extreme close-up of the aged hands at its center.
FIRST FRAME AND BLOCKING Frame one shows the full watch filling 80% of frame width, dial at x 55%, y 50%, tilted slightly toward camera against a near-black void with faint warm falloff. Weathered fingers grip the case at frame left and lower right, out of focus. Crown points screen-right. Time reads 7:37, second hand near 6.
Watch: unbranded 1970s military field watch. Matte black dial speckled with dust and micro-scratches under a domed crystal. Cream aged-lume Arabic numerals 1-12, railroad minute track, small broad-arrow mark below center. Cathedral hour hand with two rounded lume lobes, pencil minute hand, thin steel second hand. Brushed steel case, worn chipped bezel, knurled crown.
FORMAT Single continuous take, no cuts, no speed ramps.
OPTICS Macro probe-lens character, 18 degree field of view narrowing into true macro. Camera starts 25 cm from the dial, ends 3 cm from the hands. Razor-thin focus tracks forward, keeping the hands sharp while numerals and case melt into warm bokeh; the final frame resolves lume grain and dust fibers.
CAMERA Slow hypnotic dolly-in along the lens axis toward dial center, constant velocity, no drift, no reframing; precision-slider smoothness, not handheld. The hands' crossing point settles just right of center, flanked by the 8 numeral and the broad arrow.
ACTION TIMING 0.0-2.0s: full dial in frame, fingers gripping; second hand ticks; push-in begins. 2.0-4.5s: fingers exit frame edges as magnification grows; numerals slide past; focus tracks the hands. 4.5-6.0s: extreme macro on the layered hands over the black dial; dust and scratches fully resolved; second hand keeps moving to the last frame.
PHYSICS Second hand advances with real mechanical cadence. Faint micro-tremor from the holding hand early on. Dust motes drift through the light near the crystal. No morphing of numerals or hands.
LIGHTING Warm tungsten-amber key from upper left, raking low so every scratch and embossed numeral casts a tiny shadow. Deep low-key: background crushed near-black, highlights only on case rim, hand edges and lume. Golden patina palette, no flat front light.
AUDIO Soft close-mic ticking, faint room tone. No music.
LOCKS No logos or readable text besides numerals and minute track. Photoreal macro, fine film grain.
```

```text
Create a full, multi-dimensional brand identity system for “Higgsfield AI,” an advanced AI platform for image, video, animation, and generative creativity. Base all visuals around the looping black S-curve logo on neon green (smooth, continuous thick curve). The identity must feel futuristic, high-tech, kinetic, expressive, human-friendly, and motion-driven. LOGO SYSTEM: Use the black looping S-curve as the core symbol of generative flow and AI motion paths. Wordmark: “Higgsfield AI” in bold geometric rounded sans serif, open spacing. Variants: icon + wordmark, stacked, icon-only, white version, neon-outline version. Rules: never warp the curve, maintain high contrast, minimum padding = 50% logo height. COLOR PALETTE: Core colors: Neon Higgs Green #D7FF28; Absolute Black #000000; Pure White #FFFFFF. Supporting: Aqua Quantum #49FFE9; Dark Graphite #1F1F1F; Signal Grey #444444; Electric Violet #A04CFF. Meaning: Neon=creativity, Black=precision, Aqua=AI signal, Violet=experimental mode. TYPOGRAPHY: Headlines in rounded geometric sans (TG Grotesk / Satoshi / Inter Rounded); body text in modern grotesk; UI text in technical mono for prompts/code. GRAPHIC LANGUAGE: All visuals derived from S-curve geometry: wave lines, curved dividers, latent-space fields, neon arcs, circular glyphs, repeating wave patterns, neon gradients, ribbon motion paths, particle flows. BRAND PERSONALITY: Creative, kinetic, intelligent, expressive, human-centered, playfully futuristic. Voice: confident, imaginative, direct. Sample lines: “Create in motion,” “Generative thinking visualized,” “AI that flows with you.” MOTION DESIGN: Logo animates as a fluid ribbon; particle formation; elastic curves. UI motion uses curved reveals, neon pulses, smooth transitions, camera-path sweeps. Add generative energy layers: particle trails, wave distortions, neon field glows. APPLICATIONS: Website hero with neon green background and faint flowing S-curve; app icon with curve centered on neon field; product screens with clean UI, neon micro-highlights, mono font prompts, curved separators. Social templates: high contrast, oversized curve textures. Merch: hoodies, stickers, laptop skins, water bottles using the loop motif. Posters: cinematic neon fields and curved-path shapes. MATERIALS & LIGHTING: Matte neon surfaces, subtle grain, polished black acrylic, soft neon edges, transparent stacked layers. Lighting with radial neon glow, soft volumetric gradients, highlights following curvature. BRAND MYTHOLOGY: The S-curve symbolizes the Higgs Field - an abstract creative fabric where ideas form and transform. Represents movement, energy pathways, expressive AI, and continuous generative flow.
```

No weld beads, no solder — only bindings, knots, wraps and tension holding it. Optics: sharp focus on the whole object, fine detail on the wire wraps and rope knots, clean realistic photographic image. LOCKS: one handmade trophy loosely shaped like the World Cup, NOT welded — built from iron rods, wire, rope and twine all bound, tied and lashed together with visible knots and wraps, patchy gold/bronze paint over raw rusty metal, on a plain gray backdrop. ### Luxury skincare commercial with per-character colour worlds — [How to Create Ultra-Realistic Videos with Seedance 4K: Guide with Ready-To-Use Prompts](Seedance-4k.md) P13
- Source: https://higgsfield.ai/blog/Seedance-4k | Model: Seedance 2.0 (4K) | Settings: multiple references, film burn

```text
Style: 8K photorealistic editorial luxury skincare commercial, cinematic and moody, deep black crush, per-character colour worlds. The only stylized transition is a single FILM BURN from Section 7 into Section 8; all other cuts are hard match-cuts on action, sharp and instant. SCENE CONTEXT: BEGIM skincare campaign — 15-second continuous sequence, 8 internal sections. Three women each interact with BEGIM products, resolving on a hero packshot. 16:9. ACTIVE REFERENCES: @image_1 — character A: East Asian woman, short black bob, sparkly dark halter dress, on red-lit stairs with cyan backlight. @image_2 — character B: woman in a beige bikini top and flowing skirt, wide sun hat, paddleboarding on turquoise water. @image_3 — character C: woman with dark braids, red heart-shaped goggles on forehead, upside-down angle, neon cyan-pink glow, red heart balloon. @image_4 — BEGIM lineup: Hydra Memory cream jar, green Serum dropper bottle, frosted pump bottle, on black silk with lime-green fabric. Logos read exactly BEGIM, HYDRA MEMORY, SERUM. FORMAT MODE: Timed multishot, HARD CUTs on action. The single FILM BURN occurs only at 13.0s. [Section 1 — 0.0–2.0s] CHARACTER A, red-lit stairs. She pulls the BEGIM Serum dropper from a black clutch. ECU of fingers unscrewing the gold cap, cyan backlight on the green glass, one luminous drop hanging from the tip. Camera crash-zooms into the drop. [Section 2 — 2.0–4.0s] CHARACTER B, aerial drone. CU of her hands opening the Hydra Memory jar, scooping cream. Extreme macro: cream spreads across her collarbone in slow motion, golden-hour light, water droplets mixing with white cream. [Section 3 — 4.0–6.5s] CHARACTER C, upside-down, neon. She presses the frosted pump bottle — mist sprays across her face in slow motion. ECU: micro-droplets catch neon pink and cyan like prisms. [Section 4 — 6.5–8.0s] PRODUCT MONTAGE — four rapid macro cuts, 0.3s each with a strobe flash: dropper releasing serum onto a fingertip; cream swirl inside the jar; pump pressing in slow-mo; all three bottles lined up. [Section 5 — 8.0–9.5s] CHARACTER A — ECU of serum patted into the cheekbone, green-gold liquid absorbing, pores and fine texture, red staircase light pulsing. [Section 6 — 9.5–11.0s] CHARACTER B — CU of cream gliding down the forearm, turquoise water reflecting; slow dolly to her face, eyes closed in bliss, sun flare. [Section 7 — 11.0–13.0s] CHARACTER C — she catches the heart balloon string, the pump bottle against her chest; neon shifts pink→cyan→white; she looks into the lens. Slow push-in to her eyes. FILM BURN (13.0s, 10–14 frames, hard in and out): an amber-orange chemical burn ignites from the edges and crawls inward, emulsion bubbling, sprocket holes flashing, charring to embers, then burning through to Section 8. [Section 8 — 13.0–15.0s] HERO PACKSHOT — all three products on black silk with lime-green fabric. Slow dolly-in, products in the right third, clean dark negative space left, gold dust drifting, final micro-bounce settle. Hold. AUDIO: glass clink, wet cream texture, water splash, pump hiss, spray mist, ambient bass drone. Vinyl crackle and ember sizzle only on the film burn. No voiceover, no music, no on-screen text. POSITIVE LOCKS: each character and the products match their references exactly; logos stay sharp: BEGIM, HYDRA MEMORY, SERUM. Serum stays green, cream stays white. All internal cuts are hard match-cuts. The FILM BURN happens only once, at 13.0s.
```

### BAD: mountain bike commercial, no shot jobs — [Common AI Video Prompt Mistakes (And the Fixes That Actually Work)](ai-video-prompt-mistakes.md) P11
- Source: https://higgsfield.ai/blog/ai-video-prompt-mistakes | Model: any video model (BAD example) | Settings: 11 shots ~15 s

```text
SCENE: Epic mountain bike commercial, studio shots then city then forest then mountains. Eleven shots, about 15 seconds, insanely cinematic. SUBJECT AND IDENTITY: A beautiful green mountain bike with black parts and a cool rider in casual clothes. Premium product, 8k, hyperrealistic, best quality. SHOTS: 0:00-0:02 macro shots of the chain and the tire. 0:02-0:03 the bike in a clean studio. 0:03-0:05 rider riding fast in the city, then in the forest. 0:05-0:09 he rides down the trail, wheel hits a root, epic action. 0:09-0:11 close ups of the brake and the gears. 0:11-0:15 he rides a mountain road and stands on the summit at sunset. Whatever angle looks coolest for each shot. ANATOMY AND BIOMECHANICS: Close up of a hand squeezing the brake lever, fingers wrapping tightly around the metal. Legs pedaling furiously, blurred fast motion of the legs, dynamic energetic cycling. PHYSICS: Wheels smashing through roots and rocks, huge explosions of dirt and dust flying everywhere, epic particles, dramatic energy in every shot. CONTINUITY: Show the bike from lots of different angles and directions to keep it dynamic, jump between locations for variety. LIGHTING: Beautiful cinematic lighting in every shot, gorgeous sun flares, moody atmosphere, epic golden hour vibes. AUDIO: Energetic music, bike sounds, epic sound design. ### GOOD: mountain bike commercial, one job per shot — [Common AI Video Prompt Mistakes (And the Fixes That Actually Work)](ai-video-prompt-mistakes.md) P12
```
- Source: https://higgsfield.ai/blog/ai-video-prompt-mistakes | Model: Seedance / Cinema Studio | Settings: 11 segments, 10 hard cuts, ~15 s

```text
SCENE: Commercial spot for one mountain bike, studio detail shots, then city, forest trail, mountain switchback and a final hero shot. Eleven segments, ten hard cuts, about 15 seconds, real-time. SUBJECT AND IDENTITY: One bike only, olive-green frame, black components, matte finish, brand engraving on the top tube, knobby tyres, disc brakes. Frame geometry, shock mount, cable routing and decal placement stay pixel-identical in the studio shots and in every riding shot. Rider, man 30, white tee, olive overshirt, dark trousers, white trainers, same clothing throughout. SHOTS: 1. Macro pan across chain and chainring, teeth engaging link by link. 2. Macro dolly along the tyre tread. 3. Studio side tracking of the whole bike on a plain light grey cyclorama, bars turned slightly camera-right. 4. Low wide, rider riding screen-right to screen-left on city asphalt against the sun. 5. Chase from behind and above as he drops into a forest trail. 6. Low close-up of the rear wheel rolling over a tree root. 7. Medium tracking of the descent, still screen-right to screen-left. 8. Detail of the hand squeezing the brake lever. 9. Detail of the derailleur shifting across the cassette. 10. Rear three-quarter as he leans into a switchback. 11. Wide, rider standing beside the bike on the summit, back to camera, long held final beat. ANATOMY AND BIOMECHANICS: On the brake shot the hand keeps four separate fingers wrapped around the lever with visible knuckles and nail edges, the index and middle finger pulling while the thumb stays hooked under the grip; no finger sinks into or fuses with the metal. Pedalling is biomechanically correct, soles stay fixed on the pedals, ankles articulate, cadence matches the wheel speed, legs never intersect the frame or slide free of the cranks. Through the switchback the outside pedal is down and loaded, the inside pedal up, feet still. The rider's body works constantly, knees and elbows absorb impacts, hips shift back on the drop, shoulders steer, torso moves relative to the bike. PHYSICS: Tyres deform against roots and rocks and roll over them with real contact; nothing passes through solid geometry. Dirt, gravel and dust are three-dimensional particles with weight, thrown off along the tangent of wheel rotation, arcing and falling under gravity, settling on the ground. Wheel axles rotate on a fixed centre with no jitter or position jumps. Suspension compresses and rebounds visibly on every impact. CONTINUITY: The rider travels screen-right to screen-left in all riding shots. The location changes only where the terrain plausibly connects, city to forest edge to mountain road. Asphalt tone, texture and grade match across the cut between the wheel detail and the wide switchback. Background geometry, foliage, rocks and roadside stay rigid and stable during fast pans, no melting, no shifting textures. LIGHTING: Studio shots, soft even diffused light, clean grey background, controlled contrast. City, overcast daylight, low sun behind the rider. Forest, warm side and back light broken by the canopy. Mountain, hard midday sun with defined shadows. Final shot, low golden sun behind the rider. Each location keeps one consistent motivated direction. CAMERA: Macro shots at 29 degree field of view with shallow focus; riding shots 47 to 84 degree, camera 0.5 to 3 m; smooth dolly, crane and vehicle-tracking moves, shutter 270-360 degrees, natural motion blur, no speed ramps. AUDIO: Freehub ratchet, tyre hum on asphalt, gravel crunch and root impact, brake lever click, crisp derailleur shift, wind on the descent. Each effect lands exactly on its visible action, over a driving music bed cut to the edit. LOOK: Photoreal, shot on ARRI Alexa 35, natural anamorphic character, deep greens and earth tones, controlled saturation, fine grain, matte paint, machined metal, real rubber. No text, no logos beyond the bike engraving, no watermarks.
```

Lighting: surrealistic alien twilight. High-contrast natural light through a dense canopy. Underlit by vibrant bioluminescent glow from the fungal forest. Color: 60:30:10 — dominant deep indigo and violet 60% / secondary neon magenta, cyan, toxic green 30% / accent warm amber-gold (hoverbike dashboard) 10%. Camera: wide-angle cine lens (24mm). 180° shutter motion blur. Speed-ramping (24fps to 240fps and back). Extreme macro depth-of-field transition. Physics: inertia and wind resistance felt through the camera. Particles and spores obey aerodynamic drag. SUBJECTS: @rider_pov: pure first-person vision, no helmet, no HUD. At the bottom of the frame the rider's hands grip the handlebars of a heavy hoverbike, fingers adjusting the throttle. @alien_fly: a surreal iridescent macro-insect — double-layered translucent oil-slick wings, a neon-cyan segmented body pulsing with bioluminescence, large multi-faceted magenta eyes. LOCATION: a bizarre fantasy jungle at twilight, towering twisted mega-mushrooms pulsing with toxic green and magenta light. Glowing spores drift like ash. ACTION — ONE CONTINUOUS TAKE, NO cuts. 15 seconds. 0:00–0:04 — High-velocity real-time POV (24fps): the hoverbike flies low and fast, weaving through a narrow opening; the camera tilts into turns; spores streak past. 0:04–0:05 — The jolt: as the bike clips a glowing stalk, the @alien_fly flashes in from camera-left — a sudden jump-scare. The camera flinches (a micro-jerk back and right). Time violently freezes into extreme slow-motion. 0:05–0:11 — Ultra slow-motion macro (240fps): near standstill. The @alien_fly drifts past the lens at macro distance, centimeters from the eye — every microscopic detail visible, oil-slick wings scattering the neon light, spores parting around it, horizontal anamorphic magenta flares across the lens. 0:11–0:15 — Instant speed-ramp back (24fps): the fly snaps past camera-right and vanishes; time cracks back with a rush of wind, the engine roars, the rider corrects and accelerates deeper into the forest. CONSTRAINTS: 16:9. ONE CONTINUOUS POV SHOT — no cuts. No screen borders or helmet graphics. Photoreal throughout. AUDIO (NO MUSIC): heavy mechanical hum and high-RPM whine, high-speed wind. At 0:04, a caught breath. During slow-mo, wind drops into a muffled vacuum replaced by the deep thump-thump of the fly's wings and the rider's heartbeat. At 0:11, a sonic whoosh as reality snaps back.

The camera snaps into a macro of the blade igniting, embers crawling along the steel, then orbits fast as fire races up his arm and engulfs his whole body. He launches forward and the camera tracks low beside him through burning spray as he drives the flaming blade up into the Cardinal's chest; the camera pushes into a macro of the cracked mask and candle crown catching alight while the boss bar drains. The camera drops and arcs wide, ramping to slow motion as the crimson robes ignite and the colossus buckles forward, gold vestments peeling into fire and debris raining into the teal water, the boss bar emptying behind the small burning silhouette of the swordsman.

### Fix non-terminating scroll animations — [How to Build a Website with AI in 2026: From Prompt to Live URL](build-website-with-ai.md) P4
- Source: https://higgsfield.ai/blog/build-website-with-ai | Model: Higgsfield Supercomputer | Settings: fix message

```text
The scroll animations never finish. Elements start moving and then get left partway through: rotations stop short of their final angle, headlines drift toward their position and keep easing without ever landing.
Give every scroll animation a defined end state that is always reached. Pin each animated section for the full duration of its animation, so scroll progress runs from 0 to 1 before the section leaves the viewport. Every rotation must land on an exact final value with no residual offset. Replace any asymptotic smoothing with easing that terminates: if you use a lerp, add a snap threshold so the value locks to its target once it is close.
Then verify every section at three scroll speeds: slow, normal, and a fast flick. In all three the animation must end in the same final state, and all text must be readable the moment the section lands.
```
